\frac{\sqrt{n}(V_{n}^{\rm cf}-V_{n,00}^{\rm cf})}{\sigma_{n,\rm NDE}^{\rm cf,het}}=\frac{\sqrt{n}}{2\sigma_{n,\rm NDE}^{\rm cf,het}}\sum_{j=1}^{2}\left\{(\hat{\tau}_{1}^{(j)})^{\top}{\bm{\epsilon}}^{(j)}+(\hat{\tau}_{2}^{(j)})^{\top}(\mathbf{U}^{(j)}(\gamma_{1}-\gamma_{0})-{\bm{\epsilon}}^{{}^{\prime}{(j)}})\right\}

Recall that definition of
\hat{\tau}_{1}^{(j),0}
as introduced in the proof of Theorem
5.9
. We have established in Lemma
B.1
that
\sqrt{n}\|\hat{\tau}_{1}^{(j)}-\hat{\tau}_{1}^{(j),0}\|_{2}=o_{p}(1)
. Define
T^{0}
as:

T^{0}=\frac{\sqrt{n}}{2\sigma_{n,\rm NDE}^{\rm cf,het,0}}\sum_{j=1}^{2}\left[\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\in\mathcal{D}_{j}\\
A_{i}=1\end{subarray}}\hat{\tau}^{(j),0}_{1,i}{\epsilon}_{i}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\in\mathcal{D}_{j}\\
A_{i}=0\end{subarray}}\hat{\tau}^{(j)}_{2,i}(U_{i}^{\top}(\gamma_{1}-\gamma_{0})-{\epsilon}^{\prime}_{i})\right]:=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\zeta^{\rm het}_{n,i}

with

\displaystyle\zeta^{\rm het}_{n,i}

\displaystyle=\frac{\sqrt{n}}{2\sigma_{n,\rm NDE}^{\rm cf,het,0}}\left\{{\epsilon}_{i}\left(\hat{\tau}^{(1),0}_{1,i}\mathds{1}_{i\in\mathcal{D}_{1}}+\hat{\tau}^{(2),0}_{1,i}\mathds{1}_{i\in\mathcal{D}_{2}}\right)\mathds{1}_{A_{i}=1}\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\left.+(U_{i}^{\top}(\gamma_{1}-\gamma_{0})-{\epsilon}^{\prime}_{i})\left(\hat{\tau}^{(1)}_{2,i}\mathds{1}_{i\in\mathcal{D}_{1}}+\hat{\tau}^{(2)}_{2,i}\mathds{1}_{i\in\mathcal{D}_{2}}\right)\mathds{1}_{A_{i}=0}\right\}

\displaystyle(\sigma_{n,\rm NDE}^{\rm cf,het,0})^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{n}{4}\sum_{j=1}^{2}\left[\sum_{i\in\mathcal{D}_{j},A_{i}=1}(\hat{\tau}_{1,i}^{(j),0})^{2}\nu_{1}(X_{i},M_{i})+\sum_{i\in\mathcal{D}_{j},A_{i}=0}(\hat{\tau}_{2,i}^{(j)})^{2}\omega_{0}(X_{i})\right]\,.

Define
\mathcal{H}_{n}=\sigma(A_{1:n},X_{1:n},\{M_{i}:A_{i}=1\})
. Then it is immediate that conditional on
\mathcal{H}_{n}
,
\zeta^{\rm het}_{i}
are independent across
i
with
\mathbb{E}[\zeta^{\rm het}_{i}\mid\mathcal{H}_{n}]=0
and

\displaystyle{\sf var}(T^{0}\mid\mathcal{H}_{n})

\displaystyle=\sum_{i}{\sf var}(\zeta^{\rm het}_{i}\mid\mathcal{H}_{n})

\displaystyle=\frac{n}{4(\sigma_{n,\rm NDE}^{\rm cf,het,0})^{2}}\sum_{j=1}^{2}\left[\sum_{i\in\mathcal{D}_{j},A_{i}=1}(\hat{\tau}_{1,i}^{(j),0})^{2}\nu_{1}(X_{i},M_{i})+\sum_{i\in\mathcal{D}_{j},A_{i}=0}(\hat{\tau}_{2,i}^{(j)})^{2}\omega_{0}(X_{i})\right]=1\,.

The proof that
\sum_{i}\mathbb{E}[|\zeta^{\rm het}_{n,i}|^{3}\mid\mathcal{H}_{n}]=o_{p}(1)
is same as that of
\sum_{i}\mathbb{E}[|\zeta_{n,i}|^{3}\mid\mathcal{H}_{n}]=o_{p}(1)
in the proof of Theorem
5.9
upon using

\displaystyle\nu_{1}(x,m)\vee\omega_{0}(x)\leq C,\hskip 18.49988pt

\displaystyle(\text{Assumption }\ref{assm:cov_err})

\displaystyle(\gamma_{1}-\gamma_{0})^{\top}\Sigma_{U,0}(\gamma_{1}-\gamma_{0})\leq\lambda_{U,0,+}\|\gamma_{1}-\gamma_{0}\|_{2}^{2}\leq 2C_{\gamma}^{2}\lambda_{U,0,+}\,.\hskip 18.49988pt

\displaystyle(\text{Assumption }\ref{assm:cov_err}\ \ \&\ \ \ref{assm:params})

Furthermore, as we have already argued
\sigma_{n,\rm NDE}^{\rm cf,het}
is bounded away from
0
with probability going to
1
, the same calculation as of Equation (
B.14
) yields that
\sigma_{n,\rm NDE}^{\rm cf,het,0}
is also bounded away from
0
with probability going to
1
(observe that
(\sigma_{n,\rm NDE}^{\rm cf,het})^{2}-(\sigma_{n,\rm NDE}^{\rm cf,het,0})^{2}
is same as
(\sigma_{n}^{\rm cf,het})^{2}-(\sigma_{n}^{\rm cf,het})^{2}
).
Therefore,
T^{0}\xrightarrow{d}\mathcal{N}(0,1)
. Finally defining
T=\sqrt{n}(V_{n}^{\rm cf}-V_{n,00}^{\rm cf})/\sigma_{n,\rm NDE}^{\rm cf,het}
, all we need to argue is
T-T^{0}
.
This is also very similar to that of Theorem
5.9
and Corollary
5.12
.
To keep the notation consistent with the previous proofs, let us define
L^{0}=T^{0}\sigma_{n,\rm NDE}^{\rm cf,het,0}
and
L=T\sigma_{n,\rm NDE}^{\rm cf,het}
.
Similar decomposition as in Equation (
B.9
) yields:

T-T^{0}=\frac{L}{\sigma_{n,\rm NDE}^{\rm cf,het}}-\frac{L^{0}}{\sigma_{n,\rm NDE}^{\rm cf,het,0}}=\frac{L-L^{0}}{\sigma_{n,\rm NDE}^{\rm cf,het}}+\frac{L^{0}}{\sigma_{n,\rm NDE}^{\rm cf,het,0}}\left(\frac{\sigma_{n}^{\rm cf,het,0}}{\sigma_{n,\rm NDE}^{\rm cf,het}}-1\right):=T_{1}+T_{2}\,.

Therefore, to establish asymptotic normality of
T
all we need to show is
L-L^{0}=o_{p}(1)
and
|\sigma_{n,\rm NDE}^{\rm cf,het,0}-\sigma_{n,\rm NDE}^{\rm cf,het}|=o_{p}(1)
(as this along with the fact that
\sigma_{n,\rm NDE}^{\rm cf,het}
is bounded away from
0
with probability going to
1
implies
|(\sigma_{n,\rm NDE}^{\rm cf,het,0}/\sigma_{n,\rm NDE}^{\rm cf,het})-1|=o_{p}(1)
.
The second part can be proved immediately by the same calculation as in Equation (
B.14
).
For the first part, define
\mathcal{G}_{n}=\sigma(A_{1:n},X_{1:n},M_{1:n})
.
Then we have:

\textstyle L-L^{0}=\frac{\sqrt{n}}{2}\sum_{j=1}^{2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\in\mathcal{D}_{j}\\
A_{i}=1\end{subarray}}(\hat{\tau}_{1,i}^{(j)}-\hat{\tau}_{1,i}^{(j),0}){\epsilon}_{i}\,.

As a consequence,

\textstyle\mathbb{E}\left[(L-L^{0})^{2}\mid\mathcal{G}_{n}\right]\leq\frac{nc_{+}}{4}\sum_{j=1}^{2}\|\hat{\tau}^{(j)}_{1}-\hat{\tau}_{1}^{(j),0}\|_{2}^{2}=o_{p}(1)

where the conclusion follows from Lemma
B.1
. Therefore, an application of the Markov inequality yields that
L-L^{0}=o_{p}(1)
. This completes the proof.

The proof for asymptotic normality of
\hat{\theta}_{\rm NDE}^{\rm cf}
around
\theta_{\rm NDE}^{\rm pop}
follows the same argument as that of
Corollary
5.11
; we only highlight the required
changes. Define

\textstyle A_{n}=\sqrt{n}\left(\hat{\theta}_{\rm NDE}^{\rm cf}-\theta_{\rm NDE}\right),\hskip 18.49988ptB_{n}=\sqrt{n}\left(\theta_{\rm NDE}-\theta_{\rm NDE}^{\rm pop}\right).

Since
\theta_{\rm NDE}=\bar{X}^{\top}\Lambda_{\rm NDE}
and
\theta_{\rm NDE}^{\rm pop}=\mu_{X}^{\top}\Lambda_{\rm NDE}
, we have

\textstyle B_{n}=\sqrt{n}(\bar{X}-\mu_{X})^{\top}\Lambda_{\rm NDE}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(X_{i}-\mu_{X})^{\top}\Lambda_{\rm NDE}.

Consequently,
B_{n}
is measurable with respect to
\sigma(X_{1:n})=\mathcal{X}_{n}
. Similar argument as in Lemma
B.2
yields,
for every fixed
u\in{\mathbb{R}}
,

\textstyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\sf exp}\left\{iu\frac{A_{n}}{\sigma_{n,\rm NDE}^{\rm cf,het}}\right\}\,\middle|\,\mathcal{X}_{n}\right]\xrightarrow{p}e^{-u^{2}/2}.

Since
B_{n}
is
\mathcal{X}_{n}
-measurable, the same conditional characteristic-function calculation as in the proof of Corollary
5.11
gives, for any
u,v\in{\mathbb{R}}
,

\displaystyle\left|\mathbb{E}\left[{\sf exp}\left\{iu\frac{A_{n}}{\sigma_{n,\rm NDE}^{\rm cf,het}}+ivB_{n}\right\}\right]-e^{-u^{2}/2}\mathbb{E}[e^{ivB_{n}}]\right|\longrightarrow 0.

(B.16)

Since
\sigma_{X,\rm NDE}^{2}\leq C
and

\bar{\omega}_{n,\rm NDE}^{\rm het}\in[0,1]
, given any subsequence, there exists a further
subsequence, which we continue to index by
n
, such that
\sigma_{X,\rm NDE}^{2}\to\zeta^{2}
and
\bar{\omega}_{n,\rm NDE}^{\rm het}\to\omega
simultaneously along that subsequence for some
0\leq\zeta^{2}\leq C_{X}
and
0\leq\omega\leq 1
.
By the assumed asymptotic determinism,
\omega_{n,\rm NDE}^{\rm het}\to\omega
in probability along this subsequence.
First assume
\zeta>0
. By the Lindeberg condition and Equation (
B.16
),

\textstyle\left(\frac{A_{n}}{\sigma_{n,\rm NDE}^{\rm cf,het}},\frac{B_{n}}{\sigma_{X,\rm NDE}}\right)\xrightarrow{d}(Z_{1},Z_{2}),\ \ Z_{1},Z_{2}\xrightarrow{{\sf iid}}\mathcal{N}(0,1)\,.

Define
D_{n,\rm NDE}^{2}=(\sigma_{n,\rm NDE}^{\rm cf,het})^{2}+\sigma_{X,\rm NDE}^{2}
. Then, by Slutsky’s theorem,

\textstyle\frac{A_{n}+B_{n}}{D_{n,\rm NDE}}=\sqrt{\omega_{n,\rm NDE}^{\rm het}}\frac{A_{n}}{\sigma_{n,\rm NDE}^{\rm cf,het}}+\sqrt{1-\omega_{n,\rm NDE}^{\rm het}}\frac{B_{n}}{\sigma_{X,\rm NDE}}\xrightarrow{d}\sqrt{\omega}\,Z_{1}+\sqrt{1-\omega}\,Z_{2}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,1).

On the other hand, if
\zeta=0
, then
\mathbb{E}[B_{n}^{2}]=\sigma_{X,\rm NDE}^{2}\to 0
and consequently
B_{n}=o_{p}(1)
. Moreover, since

(\sigma_{n,\rm NDE}^{\rm cf,het})^{2}
is bounded away from zero with probability going to one,
D_{n,\rm NDE}/\sigma_{n,\rm NDE}^{\rm cf,het}\to 1
in probability. Thus,

\textstyle\frac{A_{n}+B_{n}}{D_{n,\rm NDE}}=\frac{A_{n}}{\sigma_{n,\rm NDE}^{\rm cf,het}}+o_{p}(1)\xrightarrow{d}\mathcal{N}(0,1).

We have therefore established that given any subsequence, there exists a further
subsequence along which

\textstyle\frac{A_{n}+B_{n}}{\sqrt{(\sigma_{n,\rm NDE}^{\rm cf,het})^{2}+\sigma_{X,\rm NDE}^{2}}}\xrightarrow{d}\mathcal{N}(0,1).

As the limit is the same, the convergence holds for the entire sequence. This completes the proof.
∎

B.6
Proof of Theorem
5.14

Proof.

On the treatment arm, the reduced form of the regression model satisfies
Y_{i}=X_{i}^{\top}\rho_{t}+\xi_{1,i}
where
\rho_{t}=\beta_{1}+\mathbf{B}_{1}^{\top}\gamma_{1}
and
\xi_{1,i}=\gamma_{1}^{\top}U^{\prime}_{i}+{\epsilon}_{i}
. Recall the definition of
\hat{\theta}_{11}^{\rm cf}
(Algorithm
3
):

\displaystyle\hat{\theta}_{11}^{\rm cf}=\frac{\hat{\theta}_{11}^{(1)}+\hat{\theta}_{11}^{(2)}}{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j=1}^{2}\left(\bar{X}^{\top}\hat{\rho}_{t}^{(j)}+(\hat{\omega}^{(j)})^{\top}(\mathbf{y}_{t,j}-\mathbf{X}_{t,j}\hat{\rho}_{t}^{(j)})\right)

\displaystyle=\theta_{11}+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j=1}^{2}\left((\bar{X}-\mathbf{X}_{t,j}^{\top}\hat{\omega}^{(j)})^{\top}(\hat{\rho}_{t}^{(j)}-\rho_{t})\right)+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j=1}^{2}(\hat{\omega}^{(j)})^{\top}{\bm{\xi}}^{(j)}_{1}

\displaystyle:=\theta_{11}+\Delta_{n,11}^{\rm cf}+V_{n,11}^{\rm cf}.

As a consequence, we have:

\displaystyle\frac{\sqrt{n}(\hat{\theta}_{\rm NIE}^{\rm cf}-\theta_{\rm NIE})}{\sigma_{n,\rm NIE}^{\rm cf,het}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\sqrt{n}((\hat{\theta}_{11}^{\rm cf}-\hat{\theta}_{10}^{\rm cf})-(\theta_{11}-\theta_{10}))}{\sigma_{n,\rm NIE}^{\rm cf,het}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\sqrt{n}(\Delta_{n,11}^{\rm cf}-\Delta_{n}^{\rm cf})}{\sigma_{n,\rm NIE}^{\rm cf,het}}+\frac{\sqrt{n}(V_{n,11}^{\rm cf}-V_{n}^{\rm cf})}{\sigma_{n,\rm NIE}^{\rm cf,het}}

where
(\Delta_{n}^{\rm cf},V_{n}^{\rm cf})
are same as defined in Equation (
B.2
). The proof structure is closely related to that of Theorem
5.9
and Theorem
5.13
. First, we show that the bias term
\sqrt{n}(\Delta_{n,11}^{\rm cf}-\Delta_{n}^{\rm cf})=o_{p}(1)
. Then we argue
\sigma_{n,\rm NIE}^{\rm cf,het}
is bounded away from
0
with probability going to
1
, and finally we show that the second term on the right-hand side of the above equation converges to
\mathcal{N}(0,1)
. For the first part, all we need to show is
\sqrt{n}\Delta_{n,11}^{\rm cf}=o_{p}(1)
as we have already established in the proof of Theorem
5.9
that
\sqrt{n}\Delta_{n}^{\rm cf}=o_{p}(1)
. Towards that end, first observe that, by Assumption
5.3
and
5.4

\xi_{1,i}=\gamma_{1}^{\top}U_{i}^{\prime}+{\epsilon}_{i}
is uniformly sub-Gaussian conditional on
\sigma(X_{1:n})
. Also, by Assumption
5.4
,
\|\rho_{t}\|_{0}=\|\beta_{1}+\mathbf{B}_{1}^{\top}\gamma_{1}\|_{0}\leq k_{\beta}+sk_{\gamma}\leq(1+s)k
. Furthermore, by Assumption
5.3
and Remark
5.7
, the design matrix
\mathbf{X}_{t,j}
satisfies RE condition with probability going to
1
.
Therefore, by standard Lasso concentration, we have:

\textstyle\|\hat{\rho}_{t}^{(j)}-\rho_{t}\|_{1}=O_{p}\left(sk\sqrt{\frac{\log{p}}{n}}\right),\hskip 9.24994pt\|\hat{\rho}_{t}^{(j)}-\rho_{t}\|_{2}=O_{p}\left(\sqrt{\frac{sk\log{p}}{n}}\right)\,.

As a consequence, we have:

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}|\Delta_{n,11}^{\rm cf}|

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{n}\sum_{j=1}^{2}\left|(\bar{X}-\mathbf{X}_{t,j}^{\top}\hat{\omega}^{(j)})^{\top}(\hat{\rho}_{t}^{(j)}-\rho_{t})\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{n}\sum_{j=1}^{2}\|\bar{X}-\mathbf{X}_{t,j}^{\top}\hat{\omega}^{(j)}\|_{\infty}\|\hat{\rho}_{t}^{(j)}-\rho_{t}\|_{1}

\displaystyle=O_{p}\left(\sqrt{n}\times\sqrt{\frac{\log{p}}{n}}\times sk\sqrt{\frac{\log{p}}{n}}\right)=o_{p}(1)\,.

where, in the last conclusion we have used
(sk\log{p})/\sqrt{n}=o(1)
(Assumption
5.4
). Furthermore,
\|\bar{X}-\mathbf{X}_{t,j}^{\top}\hat{\omega}^{(j)}\|_{\infty}=O_{p}(\sqrt{\log{p}/n})
follows from the definition of the optimization problem (
3.3
). This establishes the first part. Now, to argue
\sigma_{n,\rm NIE}^{\rm cf,het}
is bounded away from
0
with probability going to
1
, it is immediate from the definition of
\sigma_{n,\rm NIE}^{\rm cf,het}
that

\textstyle(\sigma_{n,\rm NIE}^{\rm cf,het})^{2}\geq\frac{n}{4}v^{2}_{U,0,1}\|\hat{\tau}^{(1)}_{2}\|_{2}^{2}\geq\frac{\lambda_{\min}(\Sigma_{U,0})c_{\gamma}^{2}}{4}n\|\hat{\tau}^{(1)}_{2}\|_{2}^{2}

We have already argued in the proof of Theorem
5.9
that
n\|\hat{\tau}^{(1)}_{2}\|_{2}^{2}
bounded away from
0
with probability going to
1
(see Equation (
B.6
)). Hence
\sigma_{n,\rm NIE}^{\rm cf,het}
is bounded away from
0
with probability going to
1
. Finally, we will establish the asymptotic normality. Towards that end, define an oracle balancing weight
\tau_{1}^{(j),*}
as:

\textstyle\tau_{1,i}^{(j),*}=\frac{1}{n_{t,j}}W_{i}^{\top}\eta^{*},\hskip 9.24994pt\eta^{*}=\Omega_{W,1}^{-1}a^{*}_{10}=\Omega_{W,1}^{-1}\begin{pmatrix}\mu_{X}\\
\mathbf{B}_{0}\mu_{X}\end{pmatrix},\hskip 9.24994pt1\leq i\leq n_{t,j}\,.

First we argue that:

\textstyle\sqrt{n}\|\hat{\tau}_{1}^{(j)}-\tau_{1}^{(j),*}\|_{2}=o_{p}(1)\,.

(B.17)

The proof is similar to the proof of Lemma
B.1
. Fix
j=2
(we choose
j=2
because it is explicitly mentioned in Algorithm
1
; the proof for
j=1
is verbatim). Recall that the main difference between
\hat{\tau}_{1}^{(2)}
is obtained by solving the optimization problem (
3.2
) with
a^{(1)}=(\bar{X}^{\top},\hat{m}_{c,1}^{\top})^{\top}
. Let
\hat{\eta}_{n}:=\hat{\eta}_{n}(a^{(1)})
be the rescaled dual of
\hat{\tau}_{1}^{(2)}
. Then from Equation (
C.6
) inside the proof of Lemma
B.1
, we have:

\displaystyle\hat{\eta}_{n}

\displaystyle={\arg\min}_{\eta}\left\{\frac{1}{n_{t}}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{t}}H_{n_{t}b_{n}}\left((\mathbf{W}_{t}\eta)_{i}\right)-\eta^{\top}a^{(1)}+\check{\lambda}_{n_{t}}\|\eta\|_{1}\right\}

\displaystyle={\arg\min}_{\eta}\ \left\{\mathcal{L}_{n,a^{(1)}}(\eta)+\check{\lambda}_{n_{t}}\|\eta\|_{1}\right\}\,.

where
n_{t}
is a shorthand for
n_{t,2}
and
\check{\lambda}_{n_{t}}=K_{1}\sqrt{\log{(p+q)}/n_{t}}
for some appropriately chosen large constant
K_{1}
. Now observe that:

\textstyle\max_{1\leq i\leq n_{t}}|W_{t,i}^{\top}\eta^{*}|\leq\|\eta^{*}\|_{1}\max_{i}\|W_{t,i}\|_{\infty}=O_{p}(\sqrt{\log{n}+\log{d}})=o(n^{1-\alpha})\,.

Therefore, all large
n
, with probability going to
1
,
H_{n_{t}b_{n}}(W_{t,i}^{\top}\eta^{*})=(W_{t,i}^{\top}\eta^{*})^{2}/2
for all
1\leq i\leq n_{t}
. Now from optimality of
\hat{\eta}_{n}
, we have:

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{n,a^{(1)}}(\hat{\eta}_{n})+\check{\lambda}_{n_{t}}\|\hat{\eta}_{n}\|_{1}\leq\mathcal{L}_{n,a^{(1)}}(\eta^{*})+\check{\lambda}_{n_{t}}\|\eta^{*}\|_{1}

Then, by the same calculation as used to obtain Equation (
C.10
) using convexity of
\mathcal{L}_{n,a^{(1)}}(\cdot)
, we conclude that
\|\hat{\eta}_{n}\|_{1}\leq 3\|\eta^{*}\|_{1}\leq 3C_{\eta}
with probability going
1
. We next claim that
\|\mathbf{W}_{t}(\hat{\eta}_{n}-\eta^{*})\|_{2}^{2}/n_{t}=o_{p}(1)
. Now, as we have argued
\|\hat{\eta}_{n}\|_{1}\leq 3C_{\eta}
with probability going to
1
, hence on this event,

\textstyle\max_{1\leq i\leq n_{t}}|W_{t,i}^{\top}\hat{\eta}_{n}|\leq\|\hat{\eta}_{n}\|_{1}\max_{i}\|W_{t,i}\|_{\infty}=O_{p}(\sqrt{\log{n}+\log{(p+q)}})=o(n^{1-\alpha})\,.

Hence, we also have on this event,
H_{n_{t}b_{n}}(W_{t,i}^{\top}\hat{\eta}_{n})=(W_{t,i}^{\top}\hat{\eta}_{n})^{2}/2
. Therefore, we have, again from the optimality of
\hat{\eta}_{n}
:

\textstyle\frac{1}{2n_{t}}\|\mathbf{W}_{t}\hat{\eta}_{n}\|_{2}^{2}-\hat{\eta}_{n}^{\top}a^{(1)}+\check{\lambda}_{n_{t}}\|\hat{\eta}_{n}\|_{1}\leq\frac{1}{2n_{t}}\|\mathbf{W}_{t}\eta^{*}\|_{2}^{2}-(\eta^{*})^{\top}a^{(1)}+\check{\lambda}_{n_{t}}\|\eta^{*}\|_{1}

Hence,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2n_{t}}\|\mathbf{W}_{t}(\hat{\eta}_{n}-\eta^{*})\|_{2}^{2}+\check{\lambda}_{n_{t}}\|\hat{\eta}_{n}\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq(\eta^{*})^{\top}\hat{\Omega}_{W,t}(\eta^{*}-\hat{\eta}_{n})+(\hat{\eta}_{n}-\eta^{*})^{\top}a^{(1)}+\check{\lambda}_{n_{t}}\|\eta^{*}\|_{1}

\displaystyle=(\eta^{*}-\hat{\eta}_{n})^{\top}(\hat{\Omega}_{W,t}\eta^{*}-a^{(1)})+\check{\lambda}_{n_{t}}\|\eta^{*}\|_{1}

\displaystyle=(\eta^{*}-\hat{\eta}_{n})^{\top}(\hat{\Omega}_{W,t}\eta^{*}-a_{10}^{*}+a^{*}_{10}-a^{(1)})+\check{\lambda}_{n_{t}}\|\eta^{*}\|_{1}

\displaystyle=(\eta^{*}-\hat{\eta}_{n})^{\top}((\hat{\Omega}_{W,t}-\Omega_{W,1})\eta^{*}+a^{*}_{10}-a^{(1)})+\check{\lambda}_{n_{t}}\|\eta^{*}\|_{1}

(B.18)

Now,

\textstyle\|(\hat{\Omega}_{W,t}-\Omega_{W,1})\eta^{*}\|_{\infty}\leq\|\eta^{*}\|_{1}\|\hat{\Omega}_{W,t}-\Omega_{W,1}\|_{\infty,\infty}\leq C_{\eta}C\sqrt{\frac{\log{p+q}}{n}}

with probability going to
1
, where the last inequality follows from the fact that the elements of
\hat{\Omega}_{W,t}
are sub-exponential and consequently an application of Bernstein’s inequality, along with a union bound, yields the conclusion. Furthermore, by same calculation as in Equation (
C.8
), we have, with probability going to
1
:

\textstyle\|a^{*}_{10}-a^{(1)}\|_{\infty}\leq C^{\prime}\sqrt{\frac{\log{(p+q)}}{n}}\,.

Hence, choosing
K_{1}
large enough, we conclude with probability going to
1
:

\textstyle\|(\hat{\Omega}_{W,t}-\Omega_{W,1})\eta^{*}\|_{\infty}+\|a^{*}_{10}-a^{(1)}\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{\check{\lambda}_{n_{t}}}{2}\,.

Therefore, going back to Equation (
B.18
), we have:

\textstyle\frac{1}{2n_{t}}\|\mathbf{W}_{t}(\hat{\eta}_{n}-\eta^{*})\|_{2}^{2}+\check{\lambda}_{n_{t}}\|\hat{\eta}_{n}\|_{1}\leq\|\hat{\eta}_{n}-\eta^{*}\|_{1}\frac{\check{\lambda}_{n_{t}}}{2}+\check{\lambda}_{n_{t}}\|\eta^{*}\|_{1}

Therfore, from the fact
\|\hat{\eta}_{n}\|_{1}\leq 3C_{\eta}
,
\|\eta^{*}\|_{1}\leq C_{\eta}
and
\check{\lambda}_{n_{t}}=O(\sqrt{\log{(p+q)/n}})
, we have:

\textstyle\frac{1}{2n_{t}}\|\mathbf{W}_{t}(\hat{\eta}_{n}-\eta^{*})\|_{2}^{2}=O_{p}\left(\sqrt{\frac{\log{(p+q)}}{n}}\right)=o_{p}(1)\,.

Now, going back to KKT condition of
\hat{\tau}_{1}^{(2)}
(see Equation (
C.4
) in the proof of Lemma
B.1
for more details):

\textstyle\hat{\tau}_{1}^{(2)}=\mathbf{W}_{t,2}\hat{\eta}^{\rm KKT}(a^{(1)})-\hat{\zeta}^{\rm KKT}(a^{(1)})=\frac{1}{n_{t}}\mathbf{W}_{t}\hat{\eta}_{n}-\hat{\zeta}^{\rm KKT}(a^{(1)})

where we used the fact
\hat{\eta}_{n}=n_{t}\hat{\eta}^{\rm KKT}
. We next argue that
\hat{\zeta}^{\rm KKT}(a^{(1)})=0
. Recall that from the definition of
\hat{\zeta}^{\rm KKT}(a^{(1)})
, it is
0
if the condition
\|\hat{\tau}^{(1)}\|_{\infty}\leq n^{-\alpha}
is strict, i.e.
\|\hat{\tau}^{(1)}\|_{\infty}<n^{-\alpha}
. Towards that end, recall the rescaled dual optimization problem (Equation (
C.5
) in the proof of Lemma
B.1
):

\textstyle(\hat{\eta}_{n},\hat{\zeta}_{n})={\arg\min}_{\eta,\zeta}\ \left\{\frac{1}{2n_{t}}\|\mathbf{W}_{t}\eta-\zeta\|_{2}^{2}-\eta^{\top}a+\check{\lambda}_{n_{t}}\|\eta\|_{1}+b_{n}\|\zeta\|_{1}\right\}

Therefore, given
\hat{\eta}_{n}
,
\hat{\zeta}_{n}
can be written as:

\textstyle\hat{\zeta}_{n,i}={\sf sign}(W_{t,i}^{\top}\hat{\eta}_{n})(|W_{t,i}^{\top}\hat{\eta}_{n}|-n_{t}^{1-\alpha})_{+},\hskip 18.49988pt1\leq i\leq n_{t}\,.

However, we have argue previously that with probabiity going to
1
,
|W_{t,i}^{\top}\hat{\eta}_{n}|\leq C\sqrt{\log{(p+q)}+\log{n}}=o(n_{t}^{1-\alpha})
and consequently on this even
\hat{\zeta}_{n,i}=0
for all
1\leq i\leq n_{t}
. As
\zeta^{\rm KKT}=\hat{\zeta}_{n}/n_{t}
, we have
\hat{\zeta}^{\rm KKT}=0
. Therefore,

\textstyle\hat{\tau}_{1}^{(2)}=\frac{1}{n_{t}}\mathbf{W}_{t,2}\hat{\eta}_{n}\implies n\|\hat{\tau}_{1}^{(2)}-\tau_{1}^{(2),*}\|_{2}^{2}=\frac{n}{n_{t}}\frac{1}{n_{t}}\|\mathbf{W}_{t,2}(\hat{\eta}_{n}-\eta^{*})\|_{2}^{2}=o_{p}(1)\,,

as
n\asymp n_{t}
. This completes the proof.

Now coming back to the proof of asymptotic normality, we can write the relevant part as:

\displaystyle T

\displaystyle:=\frac{\sqrt{n}(V_{n,11}^{\rm cf}-V_{n}^{\rm cf})}{\sigma_{n,\rm NIE}^{\rm cf,het}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\sqrt{n}}{2\sigma_{n,\rm NIE}^{\rm cf,het}}\sum_{j=1}^{2}\left[(\hat{\omega}^{(j)})^{\top}{\bm{\xi}}_{1}^{(j)}-(\hat{\tau}_{1}^{(j)})^{\top}{\bm{\epsilon}}^{(j)}-(\hat{\tau}_{2}^{(j)})^{\top}\mathbf{U}\gamma_{1}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{\sqrt{n}}{2\sigma_{n,\rm NIE}^{\rm cf,het}}\sum_{j=1}^{2}\left[(\hat{\omega}^{(j)})^{\top}\mathbf{U}^{\prime}\gamma_{1}+(\hat{\omega}^{(j)})^{\top}{\bm{\epsilon}}^{(j)}-(\hat{\tau}_{1}^{(j)})^{\top}{\bm{\epsilon}}^{(j)}-(\hat{\tau}_{2}^{(j)})^{\top}\mathbf{U}\gamma_{1}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{\sqrt{n}}{2\sigma_{n,\rm NIE}^{\rm cf,het}}\sum_{j=1}^{2}\left[(\hat{\omega}^{(j)}-\hat{\tau}_{1}^{(j)})^{\top}{\bm{\epsilon}}^{(j)}+(\hat{\omega}^{(j)})^{\top}\mathbf{U}^{\prime}\gamma_{1}-(\hat{\tau}_{2}^{(j)})^{\top}\mathbf{U}\gamma_{1}\right]:=\frac{L}{\sigma_{n,\rm NIE}^{\rm cf,het}}\,.

Now, define an oracle version
T^{0}
with replacing
\hat{\tau}_{1}^{(j)}
by
\tau^{(j),*}
:

\displaystyle T^{0}

\displaystyle=\frac{\sqrt{n}}{2\sigma_{n,\rm NIE}^{\rm cf,het,0}}\sum_{j=1}^{2}\left[(\hat{\omega}^{(j)}-\tau_{1}^{(j),*})^{\top}{\bm{\epsilon}}^{(j)}+(\hat{\omega}^{(j)})^{\top}\mathbf{U}^{\prime}\gamma_{1}-(\hat{\tau}_{2}^{(j)})^{\top}\mathbf{U}\gamma_{1}\right]:=\frac{L^{0}}{\sigma_{n,\rm NIE}^{\rm cf,het,0}}\,.

where

\displaystyle(\sigma_{n,\rm NIE}^{\rm cf,het,0})^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{n}{4}\sum_{j=1}^{2}\left[\sum_{i\in\mathcal{D}_{j},A_{i}=1}\mathbb{E}\left[(\hat{\omega}_{i}^{(j)}-\tau_{1,i}^{(j),*})^{2}\nu_{1}(X_{i},M_{i})\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}\right]\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\left.+v_{U,1,1}^{2}\|\hat{\omega}^{(j)}\|_{2}^{2}+v_{U,0,1}^{2}\|\hat{\tau}_{2}^{(j)}\|_{2}^{2}\right]\,.

Here
\mathcal{X}_{n}=\sigma(A_{1:n},X_{1:n})
. We will also use
\mathcal{H}_{n}=\sigma(A_{1:n},X_{1:n},\{M_{i}:A_{i}=1\})
and
\mathcal{G}_{n}=\sigma(A_{1:n},X_{1:n},M_{1:n})
. Clearly
\mathcal{X}_{n}\subseteq\mathcal{H}_{n}\subseteq\mathcal{G}_{n}
.
We first argue
T^{0}\xrightarrow{d}\mathcal{N}(0,1)
. Towards that end, define

\displaystyle\textstyle\zeta_{n,i}^{\rm het,NIE}

\displaystyle=\frac{\sqrt{n}}{2\sigma_{n,\rm NIE}^{\rm cf,het,0}}\sum_{j=1}^{2}\left[\left\{(\hat{\omega}^{(j)}_{i}-\tau_{1,i}^{(j),*}){\epsilon}_{i}+\hat{\omega}^{(j)}_{i}(U^{\prime}_{i})^{\top}\gamma_{1}\right\}\mathds{1}_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\in\mathcal{D}_{j}\\
A_{i}=1\end{subarray}}\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 184.99878pt\left.-\hat{\tau}_{2,i}^{(j)}U_{i}^{\top}\gamma_{1}\mathds{1}_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\in\mathcal{D}_{j}\\
A_{i}=0\end{subarray}}\right]

It is immediate that conditional on
\mathcal{X}_{n}
, we have
\mathbb{E}[\zeta_{n,i}^{\rm het,NIE}\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]=0
. This follows from the fact that conditional on
\mathcal{X}_{n}
,
\hat{\omega}^{(j)}
and
\hat{\tau}_{2}^{(j)}
are fixed. Furthermore:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\tau_{1,i}^{(j),*}{\epsilon}_{i}\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}[\tau_{1,i}^{(j),*}{\epsilon}_{i}\mid\mathcal{H}_{n}]\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\tau_{1,i}^{(j),*}\mathbb{E}[{\epsilon}_{i}\mid\mathcal{H}_{n}]\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}\right]=0\,.

Here we have used the fact that
\tau_{1}^{(j),*}
is measurable with respect to
\mathcal{H}_{n}
. Furthermore, for
i\in\mathcal{D}_{j}
with
A_{i}=1

\displaystyle{\sf var}(\zeta_{n,i}^{\rm het,NIE}\mid\mathcal{X}_{n})

\displaystyle=\frac{n}{4(\sigma_{n,\rm NIE}^{\rm cf,het,0})^{2}}\left\{\mathbb{E}[(\hat{\omega}^{(j)}_{i}-\tau_{1,i}^{(j),*})^{2}{\epsilon}^{2}_{i}\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]+\mathbb{E}[(\hat{\omega}^{(j)}_{i})^{2}((U^{\prime}_{i})^{\top}\gamma_{1})^{2}\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 138.74908pt+\left.2\mathbb{E}[(\hat{\omega}^{(j)}_{i}-\tau_{1,i}^{(j),*})(\omega^{(j)}_{i}){\epsilon}_{i}(U^{\prime}_{i})^{\top}\gamma_{1}\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]\right\}

\displaystyle=\frac{n}{4(\sigma_{n,\rm NIE}^{\rm cf,het,0})^{2}}\left\{\mathbb{E}[(\hat{\omega}^{(j)}_{i}-\tau_{1,i}^{(j),*})^{2}\nu_{1}(X_{i},M_{i})\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]+(\hat{\omega}^{(j)}_{i})^{2}\gamma_{1}^{\top}\Sigma_{U,1}\gamma_{1}\right\}

The expectation of product/cross term is
0
as
\mathbb{E}[{\epsilon}_{i}\mid\mathcal{H}_{n}]=0
and
\hat{\omega}_{i}^{(j)}
and
(U_{i}^{\prime})^{\top}\gamma_{1}
is measurable with respect to
\mathcal{H}_{n}
.
On the other hand, for
i\in\mathcal{D}_{j}
with
A_{i}=0
, we have:

\textstyle{\sf var}(\zeta_{n,i}^{\rm het,NIE}\mid\mathcal{X}_{n})=\frac{n}{4(\sigma_{n,\rm NIE}^{\rm cf,het,0})^{2}}\mathbb{E}[(\hat{\tau}_{2,i}^{(j)}U_{i}^{\top}\gamma_{1})^{2}\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]=\frac{n}{4(\sigma_{n,\rm NIE}^{\rm cf,het,0})^{2}}(\hat{\tau}_{2,i}^{(j)})^{2}\gamma_{1}^{\top}\Sigma_{U,0}\gamma_{1}\,.

Furthermore, the terms
\{\zeta_{n,i}^{\rm het,NIE}\}
are independent across
i
conditionally on
\mathcal{X}_{n}
. As a consequence,

\displaystyle{\sf var}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\zeta_{n,i}^{\rm het,NIE}\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{n}{4(\sigma_{n,\rm NIE}^{\rm cf,het,0})^{2}}\sum_{j=1}^{2}\left[\sum_{i\in\mathcal{D}_{j},A_{i}=1}\mathbb{E}\left[(\hat{\omega}_{i}^{(j)}-\tau_{1,i}^{(j),*})^{2}\nu_{1}(X_{i},M_{i})\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}\right]\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 184.99878pt\left.+v_{U,1,1}^{2}\|\hat{\omega}^{(j)}\|_{2}^{2}+v_{U,0,1}^{2}\|\hat{\tau}_{2}^{(j)}\|_{2}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=1\,.

Therefore, all we need to show is

\textstyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\zeta_{n,i}^{\rm het,NIE}\right|^{3}\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}\right]\xrightarrow{p}0\,.

To that end,

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\zeta_{n,i}^{\rm het,NIE}\right|^{3}\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{n^{3/2}}{8(\sigma_{n,\rm NIE}^{\rm cf,het,0})^{3}}\sum_{i}\sum_{j=1}^{2}\left\{\mathbb{E}\left[|(\hat{\omega}^{(j)}_{i}-\tau_{1,i}^{(j),*}){\epsilon}_{i}+\hat{\omega}^{(j)}_{i}(U^{\prime}_{i})^{\top}\gamma_{1}|^{3}\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}\right]\mathds{1}_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\in\mathcal{D}_{j}\\
A_{i}=1\end{subarray}}\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 184.99878pt\left.+|\hat{\tau}_{2,i}^{(j)}|^{3}\mathbb{E}\left[|U_{i}^{\top}\gamma_{1}|^{3}\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}\right]\mathds{1}_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\in\mathcal{D}_{j}\\
A_{i}=0\end{subarray}}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{n^{3/2}}{8(\sigma_{n,\rm NIE}^{\rm cf,het,0})^{3}}\sum_{i}\sum_{j=1}^{2}\left\{4\mathbb{E}\left[|(\hat{\omega}^{(j)}_{i}-\tau_{1,i}^{(j),*}){\epsilon}_{i}|^{3}\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}\right]\mathds{1}_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\in\mathcal{D}_{j}\\
A_{i}=1\end{subarray}}\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 46.2497pt\left.+4|\hat{\omega}^{(j)}_{i}|^{3}\mathbb{E}\left[|(U^{\prime}_{i})^{\top}\gamma_{1}|^{3}\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}\right]\mathds{1}_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\in\mathcal{D}_{j}\\
A_{i}=1\end{subarray}}+|\hat{\tau}_{2,i}^{(j)}|^{3}\mathbb{E}\left[|U_{i}^{\top}\gamma_{1}|^{3}\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}\right]\mathds{1}_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\in\mathcal{D}_{j}\\
A_{i}=0\end{subarray}}\right\}

Now from the fact
\|\gamma_{1}\|_{2}\leq C_{\gamma}
(Assumption
5.4
) and
U_{i}
are sub-Gaussian (Assumption
5.3
),
\mathbb{E}[|U_{i}^{\top}\gamma|^{3}]\vee\mathbb{E}[|(U^{\prime}_{i})^{\top}\gamma_{1}|^{3}]\leq C
. Furthermore, from uniform sub-Gaussianity of
{\epsilon}
(Assumption
5.3
), we have;
\mathbb{E}[|{\epsilon}_{i}|^{3}\mid\mathcal{H}_{n}]\leq C
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\zeta_{n,i}^{\rm het,NIE}\right|^{3}\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{Cn^{3/2}}{8(\sigma_{n,\rm NIE}^{\rm cf,het})^{3}}\sum_{i}\sum_{j=1}^{2}\left\{4\mathbb{E}\left[|\hat{\omega}^{(j)}_{i}-\tau_{1,i}^{(j),*}|^{3}\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}\right]\mathds{1}_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\in\mathcal{D}_{j}\\
A_{i}=1\end{subarray}}\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\left.+4|\hat{\omega}^{(j)}_{i}|^{3}\mathds{1}_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\in\mathcal{D}_{j}\\
A_{i}=1\end{subarray}}+|\hat{\tau}_{2,i}^{(j)}|^{3}\mathds{1}_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\in\mathcal{D}_{j}\\
A_{i}=0\end{subarray}}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{1}n^{3/2}}{8(\sigma_{n,\rm NIE}^{\rm cf,het})^{3}}\sum_{i}\sum_{j=1}^{2}\left\{|\hat{\omega}^{(j)}_{i}|^{3}\mathds{1}_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\in\mathcal{D}_{j}\\
A_{i}=1\end{subarray}}+\mathbb{E}\left[|\tau_{1,i}^{(j),*}|^{3}\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}\right]\mathds{1}_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\in\mathcal{D}_{j}\\
A_{i}=1\end{subarray}}+|\hat{\tau}_{2,i}^{(j)}|^{3}\mathds{1}_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\in\mathcal{D}_{j}\\
A_{i}=0\end{subarray}}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{1}n^{3/2}}{8(\sigma_{n,\rm NIE}^{\rm cf,het})^{3}}\sum_{j=1}^{2}\left\{\|\hat{\omega}^{(j)}\|_{2}^{2}\|\hat{\omega}^{(j)}\|_{\infty}+\|\hat{\tau}_{2}^{(j)}\|_{2}^{2}\|\hat{\tau}_{2}^{(j)}\|_{\infty}\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 184.99878pt\left.+\sum_{i}\mathbb{E}\left[|\tau_{1,i}^{(j),*}|^{3}\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}\right]\mathds{1}_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\in\mathcal{D}_{j}\\
A_{i}=1\end{subarray}}\right\}

In Lemma
5.8
we have established that
n\|\hat{\tau}_{2}^{(j)}\|_{2}^{2}=O_{p}(1)
. By the exact same argument one can prove that
n\|\hat{\omega}^{(j)}\|_{2}^{2}=O_{p}(1)
(as they are obtained from similar optimization problem with
\mathbf{X}_{c,j}
replaced by
\mathbf{X}_{t,j}
). Furthermore, by the constraint in Equation (
3.1
) (resp. (
3.3
)) we have
\|\hat{\tau}_{2}^{(j)}\|_{\infty}\leq n_{c,j}^{-\alpha}\leq Cn^{-\alpha}
(resp.
\|\hat{\omega}^{(j)}\|_{\infty}\leq n_{t,j}^{-\alpha}\leq Cn^{-\alpha}
where we have used the fact that
n_{c,j}\asymp n_{t,j}\asymp n
. Therefore, as
\alpha>1/2
, we have:

\textstyle n^{3/2}\|\hat{\omega}^{(j)}\|_{2}^{2}\|\hat{\omega}^{(j)}\|_{\infty}+n^{3/2}\|\hat{\tau}_{2}^{(j)}\|_{2}^{2}\|\hat{\tau}_{2}^{(j)}\|_{\infty}=O_{p}(n^{(-1-\alpha+3/2)})=o_{p}(1)

Next we argue that
n^{3/2}\sum_{j}\sum_{i}\mathbb{E}[|\tau_{1,i}^{(j),*}|^{3}\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]\mathds{1}_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\in\mathcal{D}_{j},A_{i}=1\end{subarray}}=o_{p}(1)
. Define
Q_{n,j}
as:

\textstyle Q_{n,j}=\frac{1}{n_{j}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{D}_{j}}\mathbb{E}\left[|W_{i}^{\top}\eta^{*}|^{3}\mathds{1}_{A_{i}=1}\mid\mathcal{X}\right]

Therefore,

\textstyle\mathbb{E}[Q_{n,j}]=\mathbb{E}\left[|W^{\top}\eta^{*}|^{3}\mathds{1}_{A=1}\right]\leq C\,.

Here we have used the fact that
W\mid A=1
is sub-Gaussian and
\|\eta^{*}\|_{2}\leq\|\eta^{*}\|_{1}\leq C_{\eta}
. Therefore,
Q_{n,j}=O_{p}(1)
. Now,

\displaystyle n^{3/2}\sum_{j}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{D}_{j}}\mathbb{E}\left[|\tau_{1,i}^{(j),*}|^{3}\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}\mathds{1}_{A_{i}=1}\right]

\displaystyle=n^{3/2}\sum_{j}\frac{1}{n_{t,j}^{3}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{D}_{j}}\mathbb{E}\left[|W_{i}^{\top}\eta^{*}|^{3}\mathds{1}_{A_{i}=1}\right]

\displaystyle=n^{3/2}\sum_{j}\frac{n_{j}}{n_{t,j}^{3}}\frac{1}{n_{j}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{D}_{j}}\mathbb{E}\left[|W_{i}^{\top}\eta^{*}|^{3}\mathds{1}_{A_{i}=1}\right]

\displaystyle=n^{3/2}\sum_{j}\frac{n_{j}}{n_{t,j}^{3}}Q_{n,j}=O_{p}(n^{-1/2})=o_{p}(1)\,.

Here the last line follows from the fact that
Q_{n,j}=O_{p}(1)
,
n_{j}\asymp n_{t,j}\asymp n
. Now, we argue that
\sigma_{n,\rm NIE}^{\rm cf,het,0}
is bounded away from
0
with probability going to
1
. This is very similar to the argument we used to prove
\sigma_{n,\rm NIE}^{\rm cf,het}
is bounded away from
0
with probability going to
1
by observing that:

\textstyle(\sigma_{n,\rm NIE}^{\rm cf,het,0})^{2}\geq(n/4)\|\hat{\tau}_{2}^{(1)}\|_{2}^{2}v^{2}_{U,0,1}\geq(\lambda_{\min}(\Sigma_{U,0})c_{\gamma}^{2}/4)\ n\|\hat{\tau}_{2}^{(1)}\|_{2}^{2}

and the fact that
n\|\hat{\tau}_{2}^{(1)}\|_{2}^{2}
is bounded away from
0
with probability going to
1
. Therefore, by conditional Lindeberg-Feller CLT, we conclude that
T^{0}\xrightarrow{d}\mathcal{N}(0,1)
. Finally, to conclude the proof, we show that
T-T^{0}=o_{p}(1)
. Using the same expansion as in Equation
B.9
, we have:

\textstyle T-T^{0}=\frac{L}{\sigma_{n,\rm NIE}^{\rm cf,het}}-\frac{L^{0}}{\sigma_{n,\rm NIE}^{\rm cf,het,0}}=\frac{L-L^{0}}{\sigma_{n,\rm NIE}^{\rm cf,het}}+\frac{L^{0}}{\sigma_{n,\rm NIE}^{\rm cf,het,0}}\left(\frac{\sigma_{n,\rm NIE}^{\rm cf,het,0}}{\sigma_{n,\rm NIE}^{\rm cf,het}}-1\right)

So to conclude
T-T^{0}=o_{p}(1)
, all we need to prove is
L-L^{0}=o_{p}(1)
and
|\sigma_{n,\rm NIE}^{\rm cf,het}-\sigma_{n,\rm NIE}^{\rm cf,het,0}|=o_{p}(1)
as these, along with the fact that
\sigma_{n,\rm NIE}^{\rm cf,het}
is bounded away from
0
with probability going to
1
concludes the proof. For the former,

\textstyle L-L^{0}=\frac{\sqrt{n}}{2}\sum_{j=1}^{2}(\tau_{1}^{(j),*}-\hat{\tau}_{1}^{(j)})^{\top}{\bm{\epsilon}}^{(j)}

Therefore,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[(L-L^{0})^{2}\mid\mathcal{G}_{n}]

\displaystyle=\frac{n}{4}\sum_{j=1}^{2}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{D}_{j},A_{i}=1}(\tau_{1,i}^{(j),*}-\hat{\tau}_{1,i}^{(j)})^{2}\nu_{1}(X_{i},M_{i})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{c_{+}}{4}\sum_{j}n\|\tau_{1}^{(j),*}-\hat{\tau}_{1}^{(j)}\|_{2}^{2}=o_{p}(1)

where the last line follows from Equation (
B.17
). For the latter,

\displaystyle\left|(\sigma_{n,\rm NIE}^{\rm cf,het})^{2}-(\sigma_{n,\rm NIE}^{\rm cf,het,0})^{2}\right|

\displaystyle=\frac{n}{4}\sum_{j=1}^{2}\left|\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\in\mathcal{D}_{j}\\
A_{i}=1\end{subarray}}\mathbb{E}[(\hat{\omega}_{i}^{(j)}-\tau_{1,i}^{(j),*})^{2}\nu_{1}(X_{i},M_{i})\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]-(\hat{\omega}_{i}^{(j)}-\hat{\tau}_{1,i}^{(j)})^{2}\nu_{1}(X_{i},M_{i})\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{n}{4}\sum_{j=1}^{2}\left|\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\in\mathcal{D}_{j}\\
A_{i}=1\end{subarray}}\mathbb{E}[(\hat{\omega}_{i}^{(j)}-\tau_{1,i}^{(j),*})^{2}\nu_{1}(X_{i},M_{i})\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]-(\hat{\omega}_{i}^{(j)}-\tau_{1,i}^{(j),*})^{2}\nu_{1}(X_{i},M_{i})\right|

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt+\frac{n}{4}\sum_{j=1}^{2}\left|\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\in\mathcal{D}_{j}\\
A_{i}=1\end{subarray}}(\hat{\omega}_{i}^{(j)}-\tau_{1,i}^{(j),*})^{2}\nu_{1}(X_{i},M_{i})-(\hat{\omega}_{i}^{(j)}-\hat{\tau}_{1,i}^{(j)})^{2}\nu_{1}(X_{i},M_{i})\right|

\displaystyle:=S_{1}+S_{2}\,.

To bound
S_{2}
,

\displaystyle S_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{nc_{+}}{4}\sum_{j=1}^{2}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{D}_{j},A_{i}=1}\left|(\hat{\omega}_{i}^{(j)}-\tau_{1,i}^{(j),*})^{2}-(\hat{\omega}_{i}^{(j)}-\hat{\tau}_{1,i}^{(j)})^{2}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{nc_{+}}{4}\sum_{j=1}^{2}\|2\hat{\omega}^{(j)}-\tau_{1}^{(j),*}-\hat{\tau}_{1}^{(j)}\|_{2}\|\tau_{1}^{(j),*}-\hat{\tau}_{1}^{(j)}\|_{2}

We have already established that
\sqrt{n}\|\tau_{1}^{(j),*}-\hat{\tau}_{1}^{(j)}\|_{2}=o_{p}(1)
(Equation (
B.17
)). Furthermore, we know that
n\|\hat{\tau}_{1}^{(j)}\|_{2}^{2}=O_{p}(1)
from Lemma
5.8
. By exact same argument, we also have
n\|\hat{\omega}^{(j)}\|_{2}^{2}=O_{p}(1)
. Now, from the definition of
\tau_{1}^{(j),*}
we have:

\textstyle n\|\tau_{1}^{(j),*}\|_{2}^{2}=\frac{n}{n_{t,j}^{2}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{D}_{j}}|W_{i}^{\top}\eta^{*}|^{2}\mathds{1}_{A_{i}=1}

Now, from the sub-Gaussianity of
W\mid A=1
, we have
\mathbb{E}[|W^{\top}\eta^{*}|^{2}\mathds{1}(A=1)]\leq C<\infty
. As a consequence,

\textstyle\frac{1}{n_{j}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{D}_{j}}|W_{i}^{\top}\eta^{*}|^{2}\mathds{1}_{A_{i}=1}=O_{p}(1)\,.

Therefore,

\textstyle n\|\tau_{1}^{(j),*}\|_{2}^{2}=\frac{nn_{j}}{n_{j,t}^{2}}\times\frac{1}{n_{j}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{D}_{j}}|W_{i}^{\top}\eta^{*}|^{2}\mathds{1}_{A_{i}=1}=O_{p}(1)\,.

Here, the last conclusion follows from
n_{j,t}\asymp n_{j}\asymp n
. Therefore,
S_{2}=o_{p}(1)
. Now we argue that
S_{1}=o_{p}(1)
. Set

\textstyle\upsilon_{i}^{(j)}=(\hat{\omega}_{i}^{(j)}-\tau_{1,i}^{(j),*})^{2}\nu_{1}(X_{i},M_{i})\mathds{1}_{A_{i}=1}-\mathbb{E}\left[(\hat{\omega}_{i}^{(j)}-\tau_{1,i}^{(j),*})^{2}\nu_{1}(X_{i},M_{i})\mathds{1}_{A_{i}=1}\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}\right]

Then,

\textstyle S_{1}=\frac{n}{4}\sum_{j}n_{j}\left|\frac{1}{n_{j}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{D}_{j}}\upsilon^{(j)}_{i}\right|\,.

Now, for any
i\in\mathcal{D}_{j}
,

\displaystyle{\sf var}\left(\upsilon_{i}^{(j)}\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[(\hat{\omega}_{i}^{(j)}-\tau_{1,i}^{(j),*})^{4}\nu_{1}^{2}(X_{i},M_{i})\mathds{1}_{A_{i}=1}\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}\right]

\displaystyle\leq 8c_{+}^{2}\left\{(\hat{\omega}_{i}^{(j)})^{4}\mathds{1}(A_{i}=1)+\mathbb{E}[(\tau_{1,i}^{(j),*})^{4}\mathds{1}(A_{i}=1)\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]\right\}

Hence,

\displaystyle{\sf var}\left(\frac{1}{n_{j}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{D}_{j}}\upsilon_{i}\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n_{j}^{2}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{D}_{j}}{\sf var}(\upsilon_{i}\mid\mathcal{X}_{n})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{8c_{+}^{2}}{n_{j}^{2}}\|\hat{\omega}^{(j)}\|_{2}^{2}\|\hat{\omega}^{(j)}\|_{\infty}^{2}+\frac{8c_{+}^{2}}{n_{j}^{2}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{D}_{j}}\mathbb{E}[(\tau_{1,i}^{(j),*})^{4}\mathds{1}(A_{i}=1)\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]

Now, we have already argued that
\|\hat{\omega}^{(j)}\|_{2}^{2}=O_{p}(n^{-1})
and by feasibility of
\|\hat{\omega}^{(j)}\|_{\infty}^{2}\leq O_{p}(n^{-2\alpha})
. Consequently
\|\hat{\omega}^{(j)}\|_{2}^{2}\|\hat{\omega}^{(j)}\|_{\infty}^{2}/n_{j}^{2}=O_{p}(n^{-3-2\alpha})
. On other other using the fact
\tau^{(j)}_{1,i}=(W_{i}^{\top}\eta^{*})/n_{t,j}
and
W\mid A=1
is sub-Gaussian along with
\|\eta^{*}\|_{2}\leq\|\eta^{*}\|_{1}\leq C_{\eta}
, we have:

\displaystyle\frac{8c_{+}^{2}}{n_{j}^{2}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{D}_{j}}\mathbb{E}[(\tau_{1,i}^{(j),*})^{4}\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]

\displaystyle=\frac{8c_{+}^{2}}{n_{j}n_{t,j}^{4}}\frac{1}{n_{j}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{D}_{j}}\mathbb{E}[|W_{i}^{\top}\eta^{*}|^{4}\mathds{1}(A_{i}=1)]=O_{p}(n_{j}^{-5})\,.

Here the last conclusion follows from the fact that
n_{t,j}\asymp n_{j}\asymp n
. Therefore,

\textstyle{\sf var}\left(\frac{1}{n_{j}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{D}_{j}}\upsilon_{i}\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}\right)=O_{p}(n^{-3-2\alpha})\implies\frac{1}{n_{j}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{D}_{j}}\upsilon_{i}=O_{p}(n^{-3/2-\alpha})\,.

Therefore,

\textstyle S_{1}=\frac{n}{4}\sum_{j}n_{j}\frac{1}{n_{j}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{D}_{j}}\upsilon^{(j)}_{i}=O_{p}(n^{2-3/2-\alpha})=O_{p}(n^{1/2-\alpha})=o_{P}(1)\,,

as
\alpha>1/2
. This completes the proof.

Finally, we establish the asymptotic normality of
\hat{\theta}_{\rm NIE}^{\rm cf}
around the population parameter
\theta_{\rm NIE}^{\rm pop}
. The proof follows the same argument as the population extensions for
\theta_{10}
and
\theta_{\rm NDE}
; therefore, we only highlight the changes specific to the NIE. To keep notational similarity, define
A_{n}=\sqrt{n}(\hat{\theta}_{\rm NIE}^{\rm cf}-\theta_{\rm NIE})
and
B_{n}=\sqrt{n}(\theta_{\rm NIE}-\theta_{\rm NIE}^{\rm pop})
. Since
\theta_{\rm NIE}=\bar{X}^{\top}\Lambda_{\rm NIE}
and
\theta_{\rm NIE}^{\rm pop}=\mu_{X}^{\top}\Lambda_{\rm NIE}
, we can write

\textstyle B_{n}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(X_{i}-\mu_{X})^{\top}\Lambda_{\rm NIE}\,.

Furthermore, it is immediate from the definition that
B_{n}
is measurable with respect to
\mathcal{X}_{n}
. A similar argument, used to prove Lemma
B.2
can be used to establish that for any
u\in{\mathbb{R}}
,

\textstyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\sf exp}\left\{iu\frac{A_{n}}{\sigma_{n,\rm NIE}^{\rm cf,het}}\right\}\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}\right]\xrightarrow{p}e^{-u^{2}/2}.

Consequently, by the same conditional characteristic-function calculation as in the proof of Corollary
5.11
, we have, for any
u,v\in{\mathbb{R}}
,

\displaystyle\left|\mathbb{E}\left[{\sf exp}\left\{iu\frac{A_{n}}{\sigma_{n,\rm NIE}^{\rm cf,het}}+ivB_{n}\right\}\right]-e^{-u^{2}/2}\mathbb{E}[e^{ivB_{n}}]\right|\longrightarrow 0\,.

(B.19)

Now, as
\omega_{n,\rm NIE}^{\rm het}
admits an asymptotically deterministic equivalent, there exists a deterministic sequence
\{\bar{\omega}_{n,\rm NIE}^{\rm het}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
with
\bar{\omega}_{n,\rm NIE}^{\rm het}\in[0,1]
such that
\omega_{n,\rm NIE}^{\rm het}-\bar{\omega}_{n,\rm NIE}^{\rm het}\to 0
in probability.
Fix an arbitrary subsequence. Since

\bar{\omega}_{n,\rm NIE}^{\rm het}\in[0,1]
and

\sigma_{X,\rm NIE}^{2}\leq C
, there exists a further subsequence, which we continue to index by
n
, such that

\textstyle\bar{\omega}_{n,\rm NIE}^{\rm het}\to\omega\in[0,1],\hskip 18.49988pt\sigma_{X,\rm NIE}^{2}\to\zeta^{2}

for some
\zeta\geq 0
. Consequently, along this subsequence,
\omega_{n,\rm NIE}^{\rm het}\to\omega
in probability.
First, assume
\zeta>0
. By Lindeberg-Feller central limit theorem (as
X_{i}
’s are sub-Gaussian and
\|\Lambda_{\rm NIE}\|_{2}\leq C_{\rm NIE}
is bounded (Assumption
5.4
),

\textstyle\frac{B_{n}}{\sigma_{X,\rm NIE}}\xrightarrow{d}\mathcal{N}(0,1).

Combining this with Equation (
B.19
) yields:

\textstyle\left(\frac{A_{n}}{\sigma_{n,\rm NIE}^{\rm cf,het}},\frac{B_{n}}{\sigma_{X,\rm NIE}}\right)\xrightarrow{d}(Z_{1},Z_{2}),\hskip 9.24994ptZ_{1},Z_{2}\overset{{\sf iid}}{\sim}\mathcal{N}(0,1)\,.

Define
D_{n,\rm NIE}^{2}=(\sigma_{n,\rm NIE}^{\rm cf,het})^{2}+\sigma_{X,\rm NIE}^{2}
. Then, by Slutsky’s theorem,

\textstyle\frac{A_{n}+B_{n}}{D_{n,\rm NIE}}=\sqrt{\omega_{n,\rm NIE}^{\rm het}}\frac{A_{n}}{\sigma_{n,\rm NIE}^{\rm cf,het}}+\sqrt{1-\omega_{n,\rm NIE}^{\rm het}}\frac{B_{n}}{\sigma_{X,\rm NIE}}\xrightarrow{d}\sqrt{\omega}\,Z_{1}+\sqrt{1-\omega}\,Z_{2}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,1).

Now, if
\zeta=0
, then it is immediate that
\mathbb{E}[B_{n}^{2}]=\sigma_{X,\rm NIE}^{2}\to 0
and consequently
B_{n}=o_{p}(1)
. Moreover, we have already established that
(\sigma_{n,\rm NIE}^{\rm cf,het})^{2}
is bounded away from zero with probability going to one. Consequently,
D_{n,\rm NIE}/\sigma_{n,\rm NIE}^{\rm cf,het}\to 1
in probability and
B_{n}/D_{n,\rm NIE}=o_{p}(1)
. Hence,

\textstyle\frac{A_{n}+B_{n}}{D_{n,\rm NIE}}=\frac{A_{n}}{\sigma_{n,\rm NIE}^{\rm cf,het}}+o_{p}(1)\xrightarrow{d}\mathcal{N}(0,1)\,.

Thus every subsequence contains a further subsequence along which

\textstyle\frac{A_{n}+B_{n}}{\sqrt{(\sigma_{n,\rm NIE}^{\rm cf,het})^{2}+\sigma_{X,\rm NIE}^{2}}}\xrightarrow{d}\mathcal{N}(0,1)\,,

and the limit is same for any subsequence. Therefore, the entire sequence converges to
\mathcal{N}(0,1)
. This completes the proof.
∎

Appendix C
Proof of Additional Lemmas

C.1
A Lemma on Sample Restricted Eigenvalue Conditions

Lemma C.1
.

Let
s_{X,n}=(1+s)k,\ \ s_{W,n}=k
.
Under Assumptions
5.3
and
5.4
, suppose

s_{X,n}\log\left(\frac{ep}{r_{X,n}}\right)=o(n),\hskip 18.49988pts_{W,n}\log\left(\frac{e(p+q)}{r_{W,n}}\right)=o(n).

Then, for any fixed cone constant
L<\infty
, there exist constants
c_{X,\rm RE},c_{W,\rm RE}>0
, independent of
(n,p,q)
, such that, with probability going to one, simultaneously over both arms and both data folds,

\inf_{\begin{subarray}{c}|S|\leq s_{X,n}\\
\delta\neq 0,\ \|\delta_{S^{c}}\|_{1}\leq L\|\delta_{S}\|_{1}\end{subarray}}\frac{\delta^{\top}\left(\mathbf{X}_{a,j}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{a,j}/n_{a,j}\right)\delta}{\|\delta\|_{2}^{2}}\geq c_{X,\rm RE},

and

\inf_{\begin{subarray}{c}|S|\leq s_{W,n}\\
\delta\neq 0,\,\|\delta_{S^{c}}\|_{1}\leq L\|\delta_{S}\|_{1}\end{subarray}}\frac{\delta^{\top}\left(\mathbf{W}_{a,j}^{\top}\mathbf{W}_{a,j}/n_{a,j}\right)\delta}{\|\delta\|_{2}^{2}}\geq c_{W,\rm RE}.

Consequently, the corresponding compatibility constants are bounded away from zero with probability tending to one.

Proof.

As the proof is the same for the first and the second conclusion of the lemma, we present the proof in a unified way. Let
V
denote either
X
or
W
. We will use the notation
d=\dim(V)
; when
V=X
then
d=p
and when
V=W
then
d=p+q
. Fix
a\in\{0,1\}
and let
\mu_{V,a}=\mathbb{E}[V\mid A=a]
and
Q_{V,a}=\mathbb{E}[VV^{\top}\mid A=a]
be the conditional mean and second moment of
V
given
A=a
. Now writing
\Sigma_{V,a}={\sf var}(V\mid A=a)
, we have
Q_{V,a}=\Sigma_{V,a}+\mu_{V,a}\mu_{V,a}^{\top}
. By Assumptions
5.3
and
5.4
, along with Remark
5.6
, there exist constants
0<c_{V}<C_{V}<\infty
(independent of
(n,p,q)
) such that:

\textstyle\lambda_{\min}(Q_{V,a})\geq c_{V},\hskip 18.49988pt\max_{1\leq\ell\leq d}(Q_{V,a})_{\ell\ell}\leq C_{V}\,.

Therefore, if we define
Z_{V,a}=Q_{V,a}^{-1/2}V
, then we have
\mathbb{E}[Z_{V,a}Z_{V,a}^{\top}\mid A=a]=\mathbf{I}_{d}
. Furthermore, conditional on
A=a
,
Z_{V,a}
is sub-Gaussian random vector as

\textstyle\|u^{\top}Z_{V,a}\|_{\psi_{2}\mid A=a}\leq\|u^{\top}Q_{V,a}^{-1/2}(V-\mu_{V,a})\|_{\psi_{2}\mid A=a}+C|u^{\top}Q_{V,a}^{-1/2}\mu_{V,a}|\leq C\|u\|_{2}\,.

Here, we have used the fact that
\lambda_{\min}(Q_{V,a})\geq c_{V}
and
\|Q_{V,a}^{-1/2}\mu_{V,a}\|_{2}^{2}\leq 1
which follows from the fact that
\mu_{V,a}\mu_{V,a}^{\top}\preceq Q_{V,a}
. Let
I_{a,j}:=\{i:i\in\mathcal{D}_{j},\ A_{i}=a\}
denotes that set of all observations with
A_{i}=a
in the
j^{th}
fold and let
\mathcal{I}_{n}=\sigma(A_{1:n})
, the sigma-field generated by the treatment indicators. It is immediate that
n_{a,j}=|I_{a,j}|
and conditionally on
\mathcal{I}_{n}
,
n_{a,j}
is fixed and the rows of
\mathbf{V}_{a,j}
are i.i.d. with the common distribution
V\mid A=a
. Hence, we have:

\textstyle\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\mathbf{V}_{a,j}^{\top}\mathbf{V}_{a,j}}{n_{a,j}}\mid\mathcal{I}_{n}\right]=\mathbb{E}[VV^{\top}\mid A=a]=Q_{V,a}.

Therefore, if we write
\mathbf{V}_{a,j}=\Psi_{V,a,j}Q_{V,a}^{1/2}
, then, conditionally on
\mathcal{I}_{n}
, where the rows of
\Psi_{V,a,j}
are independent along with second moment (conditionally on
A=a
) being
\mathbf{I}_{d}
. Therefore, an application of Theorem 15 of
Rudelson and Zhou (2012)
with
A=Q_{V,a}^{1/2}
, sparsity level
s_{V,n}
implies:

\textstyle n_{a,j}\gtrsim s_{V,n}\log\left(\frac{ed}{s_{V,n}}\right)\implies\frac{1}{n_{a,j}}\|\mathbf{V}_{a,j}\delta\|_{2}^{2}\geq c_{V,\mathrm{RE}}\|\delta\|_{2}^{2}

simultaneously for all sets
S
with
|S|\leq s_{V,n}
and all
\delta\neq 0
satisfying
\|\delta_{S^{c}}\|_{1}\leq L\|\delta_{S}\|_{1}
.
However, to apply this theorem, we need to show
Q_{V,a}^{1/2}
satisfies the RE condition. This immediately follows from our assumptions; since
Q_{V,a}=\Sigma_{V,a}+\mu_{V,a}\mu_{V,a}^{\top}\succeq\Sigma_{V,a}\succeq c_{V}\mathbf{I}_{d}
. we have, for every
\delta\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
,

\textstyle\|Q_{V,a}^{1/2}\delta\|_{2}\geq\sqrt{c_{V}}\|\delta\|_{2}.

Therefore, for every
S
with
|S|\leq s_{V,n}
and every
\delta
satisfying
\|\delta_{S^{c}}\|_{1}\leq 3L\|\delta_{S}\|_{1}
, we have
\|Q_{V,a}^{1/2}\delta\|_{2}\geq\sqrt{c_{V}}\|\delta_{S}\|_{2}
. Hence
Q_{V,a}^{1/2}
satisfies
\mathrm{RE}(s_{V,n},3L)
, with
K(s_{V,n},3L,Q_{V,a}^{1/2})\leq c_{V}^{-1/2}
.
Finally, for any
\delta
in the corresponding cone, we have
\|\delta_{S}\|_{1}^{2}\leq|S|\|\delta_{S}\|_{2}^{2}\leq s_{V,n}\|\delta\|_{2}^{2}
. Therefore,

\textstyle\frac{s_{V,n}\delta^{\top}(\mathbf{V}_{a,j}^{\top}\mathbf{V}_{a,j}/n_{a,j})\delta}{\|\delta_{S}\|_{1}^{2}}\geq c_{V,\mathrm{RE}},

so the corresponding compatibility constants are also bounded away from zero with probability going to one.
∎

C.2
Proof of Lemma
B.1

Proof.

We only prove the case for
j=2
(as it is exactly stated in Algorithm
1
), since the case for
j=1
is analogous.
For notational simplicity, we prove the result for
j=2
and suppress the fold indices throughout the proof. Define

\textstyle a:=a^{(1)}=\begin{pmatrix}\bar{X}\\
\hat{m}_{c}\end{pmatrix},\hskip 18.49988pta^{0}=\begin{pmatrix}\bar{X}\\
B_{0}\bar{X}\end{pmatrix},\hskip 18.49988pta^{*}:=a_{10}^{*}=\begin{pmatrix}\mu_{X}\\
B_{0}\mu_{X}\end{pmatrix},

where
\hat{m}_{c}=\hat{m}_{c,1}
. Thus,
a
is the actual random contrast used in Algorithm
1
,
a^{0}
is its oracle sample counterpart obtained by replacing
\hat{m}_{c}
with
B_{0}\bar{X}
, and
a^{*}
is the corresponding population contrast.
We also write
\eta^{*}:=\eta_{10}^{*}=\Omega_{W,1}^{-1}a^{*}
.
The optimization problem in Equation (
3.2
) can be cleanly written as:

\displaystyle\min_{\tau\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n_{t}}}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\|\tau\|_{2}^{2}

(C.1)

\displaystyle\text{subject to}

\displaystyle\bigl\|a-\mathbf{W}_{t}^{\top}\tau\bigr\|_{\infty}\leq\check{\lambda}_{n}:=K_{1}\sqrt{\frac{\log{(p+q)}}{n_{t}}}\,,

\displaystyle\|\tau\|_{\infty}\leq b_{n}:=n_{t}^{-\alpha}\,,

and
\hat{\tau}(a)
is the unique optimizer of the above optimization problem (it exists and is unique as the objective is strongly convex over a convex compact set).
For any
v\in\mathbb{R}^{p+q}
, let
\hat{\tau}(v)
denote the unique optimizer of Equation (
C.1
) with
a
replaced by
v
. Therefore, it is immediate that
\hat{\tau}(a)=\hat{\tau}_{2}^{(1)}
and
\hat{\tau}(a^{0})=\hat{\tau}_{2}^{(1),0}
. The Lagrangian of the above optimization problem can be written as, given non-negative vectors (co-ordinatewise)
\{\eta_{i,+}\}_{i=1}^{p+q},\{\eta_{i,-}\}_{i=1}^{p+q}
and
\{\zeta_{i,+}\}_{i=1}^{n_{t}},\{\zeta_{i,-}\}_{i=1}^{n_{t}}
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{L}\left(\tau,\{\eta_{i,+}\},\{\eta_{i,-}\},\{\zeta_{i,+}\},\{\zeta_{i,-}\}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\|\tau\|_{2}^{2}+\sum_{i=1}^{p+q}\eta_{i,+}\left((a-\mathbf{W}_{t}^{\top}\tau)_{i}-\check{\lambda}_{n_{t}}\right)+\sum_{i=1}^{p+q}\eta_{i,-}\left((\mathbf{W}_{t}^{\top}\tau-a)_{i}-\check{\lambda}_{n_{t}}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 92.49939pt+\sum_{i=1}^{n_{t}}\zeta_{i,+}\left(\tau_{i}-b_{n}\right)+\sum_{i=1}^{n_{t}}\zeta_{i,-}\left(-\tau_{i}-b_{n}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\|\tau\|_{2}^{2}+\sum_{i=1}^{p+q}(\eta_{i,+}-\eta_{i,-})(a-\mathbf{W}_{t}^{\top}\tau)_{i}-\check{\lambda}_{n_{t}}\sum_{i=1}^{p+q}(\eta_{i,+}+\eta_{i,-})

\displaystyle\hskip 92.49939pt+\sum_{i=1}^{n_{t}}(\zeta_{i,+}-\zeta_{i,-})\tau_{i}-b_{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{t}}(\zeta_{i,+}+\zeta_{i,-})

Define the signed multipliers
\eta:=\eta_{+}-\eta_{-}
, and
\zeta:=\zeta_{+}-\zeta_{-}
. For any fixed signed vector
\eta
, every nonnegative decomposition

\eta=\eta_{+}-\eta_{-}
satisfies, coordinate-wise,
\eta_{j,+}+\eta_{j,-}\geq|\eta_{j}|,
with equality for the canonical decomposition
\eta_{j,+}=(\eta_{j})_{+}
and
\eta_{j,-}=(-\eta_{j})_{+}
. Since the dual objective contains
-\check{\lambda}_{n_{t}}\mathbf{1}^{\top}(\eta_{+}+\eta_{-})
, while all its remaining terms depend on
(\eta_{+},\eta_{-})
only through
\eta_{+}-\eta_{-}
, any overlap between
\eta_{+}
and
\eta_{-}
can be removed without changing the signed multiplier and while weakly
increasing the dual objective. Hence, without loss of generality,
\mathbf{1}^{\top}(\eta_{+}+\eta_{-})=\|\eta\|_{1}.
The same argument gives
\mathbf{1}^{\top}(\zeta_{+}+\zeta_{-})=\|\zeta\|_{1}.
Consequently, the Lagrangian may be written in terms of the signed multipliers as

\mathbb{L}(\tau,\{\eta_{i}\},\{\zeta_{i}\})=\frac{1}{2}\|\tau\|_{2}^{2}+\eta^{\top}(a-\mathbf{W}_{t}^{\top}\tau)+\zeta^{\top}\tau-\check{\lambda}_{n_{t}}\|\eta\|_{1}-b_{n}\|\zeta\|_{1}\,.

(C.2)

We will also use this reduced form in our analysis. Henceforth, we will use the notation
\hat{\eta}^{\rm KKT}(a),\hat{\zeta}^{\rm KKT}(a)
to denote the dual optimal parameters of this optimization problem. From the complementary slackness of the KKT condition, we have:

\displaystyle\hat{\eta}^{\mathrm{KKT}}_{j,+}(a)\left\{(a-\mathbf{W}_{t}^{\top}\hat{\tau}(a))_{j}-\check{\lambda}_{n_{t}}\right\}

\displaystyle=0,

\displaystyle\hat{\eta}^{\mathrm{KKT}}_{j,-}(a)\left\{(\mathbf{W}_{t}^{\top}\hat{\tau}(a)-a)_{j}-\check{\lambda}_{n_{t}}\right\}

\displaystyle=0.

Similarly, for every
i=1,\ldots,n_{t}
,

\displaystyle\hat{\zeta}^{\mathrm{KKT}}_{i,+}(a)\left\{\hat{\tau}_{i}(a)-b_{n}\right\}=0,\hskip 18.49988pt\hat{\zeta}^{\mathrm{KKT}}_{i,-}(a)\left\{-\hat{\tau}_{i}(a)-b_{n}\right\}=0.

Let us first focus on
\hat{\eta}^{\rm KKT}
. From the above condition, it is immediate that when
|(a-\mathbf{W}_{t}^{\top}\hat{\tau}(a))_{j}|\neq\check{\lambda}_{n_{t}}
, then
\hat{\eta}^{\rm KKT}_{j,+}(a)=\hat{\eta}^{\rm KKT}_{j,-}(a)=0
and hence
\hat{\eta}^{\rm KKT}_{j}(a)=0
. On the other hand when
(a-\mathbf{W}_{t}^{\top}\hat{\tau}(a))_{j}=\check{\lambda}_{n_{t}}
, then
\hat{\eta}^{\rm KKT}_{j,-}(a)=0
and consequently
\eta^{\rm KKT}_{j}\geq 0
. Similarly, when
(a-\mathbf{W}_{t}^{\top}\hat{\tau}(a))_{j}=-\check{\lambda}_{n_{t}}
, then
\hat{\eta}^{\rm KKT}_{j,+}(a)=0
and consequently
\eta^{\rm KKT}_{j}\leq 0
. Therefore,
\hat{\eta}^{\rm KKT}(a)\in\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{B}_{\infty}(\check{\lambda}_{n_{t}})}(a-\mathbf{W}_{t}^{\top}\hat{\tau}(a))
, where
\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{A}}(x)
is the normal cone of
\mathcal{A}
at
x
defined as:

\textstyle\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{A}}(x)=\left\{g:\langle g,y-x\rangle\leq 0,\ \forall\ y\in\mathcal{A}\right\}\,.

(C.3)

By a similar logic, we also conclude that
\hat{\zeta}^{\rm KKT}(a)_{i}=0
for
|\hat{\tau}(a)_{i}|\neq b_{n}
,
\hat{\zeta}^{\rm KKT}(a)_{i}\geq 0
for
\hat{\tau}(a)_{i}=b_{n}
and
\hat{\zeta}^{\rm KKT}_{i}(a)\leq 0
for
\hat{\tau}(a)_{i}=-b_{n}
. Therefore,
\hat{\zeta}^{\rm KKT}(a)\in\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{B}_{\infty}(b_{n})}(\hat{\tau}(a))
.
The following lemma relates the difference
\hat{\tau}(a)-\hat{\tau}(a^{0})
to that of
(a-a^{0})
and the dual variable
(\hat{\eta}^{\rm KKT}(a)-\hat{\eta}^{\rm KKT}(a^{0}))
:

Lemma C.2
.

The primal optimizer
(\hat{\tau}(a),\hat{\tau}(a^{0}))
and the dual optimizer
(\hat{\eta}^{\rm KKT}(a)-\hat{\eta}^{\rm KKT}(a^{0}))
satisfies:

\textstyle\|\hat{\tau}(a)-\hat{\tau}(a^{0})\|_{2}^{2}\leq\|a-a^{0}\|_{\infty}\|\hat{\eta}^{\rm KKT}(a)-\hat{\eta}^{\rm KKT}(a^{0})\|_{1}\,.

Proof.

The key ingredient of the proof of this lemma is a simple fact about the normal cone: let
g\in\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{A}}(x)
and
g^{\prime}\in\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{A}}(x^{\prime})
. Then
\langle g-g^{\prime},x-x^{\prime}\rangle\geq 0
. The proof is simple: taking
y=x^{\prime}
in the definition of
g\in N_{A}(x)
and
y=x
in the definition of
g^{\prime}\in N_{A}(x^{\prime})
gives

\langle g,x^{\prime}-x\rangle\leq 0
and
\langle g^{\prime},x-x^{\prime}\rangle\leq 0
, which together imply

\langle g-g^{\prime},x-x^{\prime}\rangle\geq 0
.
From the first KKT condition, we have, from Equation (
C.2
):

\textstyle\hat{\tau}(a)-\mathbf{W}_{t}\hat{\eta}^{\rm KKT}(a)+\hat{\zeta}^{\rm KKT}(a)=0\implies\hat{\tau}(a)=\mathbf{W}_{t}\hat{\eta}^{\rm KKT}(a)-\hat{\zeta}^{\rm KKT}(a)\,.

(C.4)

For notational simplicity, define
\Delta\hat{\tau}=\hat{\tau}(a)-\hat{\tau}(a^{0})
(and analogously define
\Delta\hat{\eta}
and
\Delta\hat{\zeta}
). Then, from the above equation, we have:

\textstyle\Delta\hat{\tau}=\mathbf{W}_{t}\Delta\hat{\eta}-\Delta\hat{\zeta}\implies\|\Delta\hat{\tau}\|_{2}^{2}=(\mathbf{W}_{t}^{\top}\Delta\hat{\tau})^{\top}\Delta\hat{\eta}-\Delta\hat{\tau}^{\top}\Delta\hat{\zeta}\,.

Now,
\hat{\zeta}(a)\in\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{B}_{\infty}(b_{n})}(\hat{\tau}(a))
and
\hat{\zeta}(a^{0})\in\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{B}_{\infty}(b_{n})}(\hat{\tau}(a^{0}))
. Therefore, from the property of the normal cone described above, we have
\Delta\hat{\tau}^{\top}\Delta\hat{\zeta}\geq 0
, and consequently
\|\Delta\hat{\tau}\|_{2}^{2}\leq(\mathbf{W}_{t}^{\top}\Delta\hat{\tau})^{\top}\Delta\hat{\eta}
. Now for the next part, we rewrite:

\textstyle\mathbf{W}_{t}^{\top}\Delta\hat{\tau}=(\mathbf{W}_{t}^{\top}\hat{\tau}(a)-a)-(\mathbf{W}_{t}^{\top}\hat{\tau}(a^{0})-a^{0})+(a-a^{0})\,.

Hence,

\textstyle(\mathbf{W}_{t}^{\top}\Delta\hat{\tau})^{\top}\Delta\hat{\eta}=\left((\mathbf{W}_{t}^{\top}\hat{\tau}(a)-a)-(\mathbf{W}_{t}^{\top}\hat{\tau}(a^{0})-a^{0})\right)^{\top}\Delta\hat{\eta}+(a-a^{0})^{\top}\Delta\hat{\eta}

The first summand on the right-hand side is again negative from the property of the normal cone, and consequently:

\textstyle\|\Delta\hat{\tau}\|_{2}^{2}\leq(\mathbf{W}_{t}^{\top}\Delta\hat{\tau})^{\top}\Delta\hat{\eta}\leq(a-a^{0})^{\top}\Delta\hat{\eta}\leq\|a-a^{0}\|_{\infty}\|\hat{\eta}^{\rm KKT}(a)-\hat{\eta}^{\rm KKT}(a^{0})\|_{1}

where the last inequality follows from the Hölder inequality. This completes the proof.
∎

In the rest of the analysis, we will establish that
n\|\hat{\eta}^{\rm KKT}(a)\|_{1}\vee n\|\hat{\eta}^{\rm KKT}(a^{0})\|_{1}=O_{p}(1)
. This, along with the fact that
\|a-a^{0}\|_{\infty}=o_{p}(1)
establishes the claim. Towards that end, we will start with the reduced form Lagrangian in Equation (
C.2
). Now for any fixed
(\eta,\zeta)
,
\mathbb{L}(\tau,\{\eta_{i}\},\{\zeta_{i}\})
is minimized (with respect to
\tau
) at
\tau^{*}(\eta,\zeta)=\mathbf{W}_{t}\eta-\zeta
. As a consequence:

\displaystyle\min_{\tau}\mathbb{L}(\tau,\{\eta_{i}\},\{\zeta_{i}\})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\|\mathbf{W}_{t}\eta-\zeta\|_{2}^{2}+\eta^{\top}a-\|\mathbf{W}_{t}\eta-\zeta\|_{2}^{2}-\check{\lambda}_{n_{t}}\|\eta\|_{1}-b_{n}\|\zeta\|_{1}

\displaystyle=-\left\{\frac{1}{2}\|\mathbf{W}_{t}\eta-\zeta\|_{2}^{2}-\eta^{\top}a+\check{\lambda}_{n_{t}}\|\eta\|_{1}+b_{n}\|\zeta\|_{1}\right\}\,.

Therefore, the dual optimization problem is:

\displaystyle\max_{\eta,\zeta}\ -\left\{\frac{1}{2}\|\mathbf{W}_{t}\eta-\zeta\|_{2}^{2}-\eta^{\top}a+\check{\lambda}_{n_{t}}\|\eta\|_{1}+b_{n}\|\zeta\|_{1}\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\min_{\eta,\zeta}\ \left\{\frac{1}{2}\|\mathbf{W}_{t}\eta-\zeta\|_{2}^{2}-\eta^{\top}a+\check{\lambda}_{n_{t}}\|\eta\|_{1}+b_{n}\|\zeta\|_{1}\right\}

Now let us re-parametrize
\eta_{n}=n_{t}\eta
and
\zeta_{n}=n_{t}\zeta
.
With this reparametrization, all we need to show is
\|\hat{\eta}(a)\|_{1}\vee\|\hat{\eta}(a^{0})\|_{1}=O_{p}(1)
.
Then, we can rewrite the dual problem with this new definition of
(\eta,\zeta)
as:

\textstyle-\min_{\eta,\zeta}\ \left\{\frac{1}{2n_{t}}\|\mathbf{W}_{t}\eta-\zeta\|_{2}^{2}-\eta^{\top}a+\check{\lambda}_{n_{t}}\|\eta\|_{1}+b_{n}\|\zeta\|_{1}\right\}

(C.5)

Now, for a fixed
\eta
, the optimization over
\zeta
is simple, as the objective function is separable over coordinates:

\textstyle\min_{\zeta}\ \left\{\frac{1}{2n_{t}}\|\mathbf{W}_{t}\eta-\zeta\|_{2}^{2}+b_{n}\|\zeta\|_{1}\right\}=\min_{\zeta}\ \left\{\sum_{j=1}^{n_{t}}\left(\frac{1}{2n_{t}}((\mathbf{W}_{t}\eta)_{i}-\zeta_{i})^{2}+b_{n}|\zeta_{i}|\right)\right\}

It is well-known that for each coordinate the objective is the proximal operator of
\ell_{1}
norm and hence the minimizer
\zeta_{i}^{*}={\sf sign}((\mathbf{W}_{t}\eta)_{i})(|(\mathbf{W}_{t}\eta)_{i}|-n_{t}b_{n})_{+}
and the minimum value is the Huber function:

\min_{\zeta_{i}}\frac{1}{2n_{t}}((\mathbf{W}_{t}\eta)_{i}-\zeta_{i})^{2}+b_{n}|\zeta_{i}|=\begin{cases}\frac{(\mathbf{W}_{t}\eta)_{i}^{2}}{2n_{t}},&\text{ if }|(\mathbf{W}_{t}\eta)_{i}|\leq n_{t}b_{n}\\
b_{n}|(\mathbf{W}_{t}\eta)_{i}|-\frac{n_{t}b_{n}^{2}}{2},&\text{ if }|(\mathbf{W}_{t}\eta)_{i}|>n_{t}b_{n}\end{cases}

Therefore, if we define Huber function
H_{\lambda}(x)
as
x^{2}/2
when
|x|\leq\lambda
and
\lambda|x|-\lambda^{2}/2
for
|x|>\lambda
, then we have:

\displaystyle\min_{\eta,\zeta}\ \left\{\frac{1}{2n_{t}}\|\mathbf{W}_{t}\eta-\zeta\|_{2}^{2}-\eta^{\top}a+\check{\lambda}_{n_{t}}\|\eta\|_{1}+b_{n}\|\zeta\|_{1}\right\}

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt=\min_{\eta}\left\{\frac{1}{n_{t}}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{t}}H_{n_{t}b_{n}}\left((\mathbf{W}_{t}\eta)_{i}\right)-\eta^{\top}a+\check{\lambda}_{n_{t}}\|\eta\|_{1}\right\}

Therefore, we have:

\displaystyle\hat{\eta}_{n}(a)

\displaystyle={\arg\min}_{\eta}\left\{\frac{1}{n_{t}}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{t}}H_{n_{t}b_{n}}\left((\mathbf{W}_{t}\eta)_{i}\right)-\eta^{\top}a+\check{\lambda}_{n_{t}}\|\eta\|_{1}\right\}

\displaystyle:={\arg\min}_{\eta}\ \left\{\mathcal{L}_{n,a}(\eta)+\check{\lambda}_{n_{t}}\|\eta\|_{1}\right\}\,.

(C.6)

Furthemore, we have
\hat{\eta}_{n}(a)=n_{t}\hat{\eta}^{\rm KKT}(a)
. A similar relation holds for
a^{0}
as well. By Assumption
5.4
, we have
\|\eta^{*}\|_{1}\leq C_{\eta}
. We next bound
\|a^{0}-a^{*}\|_{\infty}
and

\|a-a^{*}\|_{\infty}
:

\displaystyle\|a^{0}-a^{*}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\max\left\{\|\bar{X}-\mu_{X}\|_{\infty},\,\|\mathbf{B}_{0}(\bar{X}-\mu_{X})\|_{\infty}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq(1+\|\mathbf{B}_{0}\|_{1,\infty})\|\bar{X}-\mu_{X}\|_{\infty}=O_{p}\left(\sqrt{\frac{\log d}{n}}\right)

(C.7)

\displaystyle\|a-a^{*}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq(1+\|\mathbf{B}_{0}\|_{1,\infty})\|\bar{X}-\mu_{X}\|_{\infty}+\|\hat{m}_{c}-\mathbf{B}_{0}\bar{X}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle=O_{p}\left(\sqrt{\frac{\log d}{n}}\right).

(C.8)

Let
\hat{\Omega}_{W,1}=(\mathbf{W}_{t}^{\top}\mathbf{W}_{t})/n_{t}
. Since the rows of
\mathbf{W}_{t}
are uniformly sub-Gaussian with bounded coordinate means, we have
\|\hat{\Omega}_{W,1}-\Omega_{W,1}\|_{\max}=O_{p}(\sqrt{\log{(p+q)}/n_{t}})
. Consequently, simultaneously for
v\in\{a,a^{0}\}
,

\textstyle\|\hat{\Omega}_{W,1}\eta^{*}-v\|_{\infty}\leq\|\hat{\Omega}_{W,1}-\Omega_{W,1}\|_{\max}\|\eta^{*}\|_{1}+\|a^{*}-v\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{\check{\lambda}_{n_{t}}}{2}

(C.9)

with probability tending to one, after choosing the fixed constant

K_{1}
is sufficiently large. Furthermore, from the sub-Gaussianity of
W_{i}
(Assumption
5.3
and Remark
5.6
), we also have

\textstyle\max_{1\leq i\leq n_{t}}|W_{i}^{\top}\eta^{*}|\leq\|\eta^{*}\|_{1}\max_{1\leq i\leq n_{t}}\|W_{i}\|_{\infty}=O_{p}\left(\sqrt{\log(nd)}\right).

Since
n_{t}b_{n}=n_{t}^{1-\alpha}\gg\sqrt{\log(nd)}
, it follows that, with probability going to one,
\max_{1\leq i\leq n_{t}}|W_{i}^{\top}\eta^{*}|<n_{t}b_{n}
and consequently the Huber loss is quadratic at
\eta^{*}
and, for
v\in\{a,a^{0}\}
,
\nabla\mathcal{L}_{n_{t},v}(\eta^{*})=\hat{\Omega}_{W,1}\eta^{*}-v
.
The same calculations also show that following the oracle balancing weight
\tau_{i}^{\circ}=(W_{i}^{\top}\eta^{*})/n_{t}
for
1\leq i\leq n_{t}
,
satisfies:

\textstyle\|v-\mathbf{W}_{t}^{\top}\tau^{\circ}\|_{\infty}\leq\check{\lambda}_{n_{t}}/2<\check{\lambda}_{n_{t}},\hskip 18.49988pt\|\tau^{\circ}\|_{\infty}=O_{p}\left(\frac{\sqrt{\log(nd)}}{n}\right)=o_{p}(b_{n}),

Now, for
v\in\{a,a^{0}\}
, let
\hat{\eta}_{n}(v)
denote the rescaled Huber-dual minimizer:

\textstyle\hat{\eta}_{n}(v)\in{\arg\min}_{\eta\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\left\{\mathcal{L}_{n,v}(\eta)+\check{\lambda}_{n_{t}}\|\eta\|_{1}\right\},

Optimality of
\hat{\eta}_{n}(v)
and convexity of

\mathcal{L}_{n,v}
imply

\displaystyle\check{\lambda}_{n_{t}}\|\hat{\eta}_{n}(v)\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\nabla\mathcal{L}_{n,v}(\eta^{*})\right\|_{\infty}\|\hat{\eta}_{n}(v)-\eta^{*}\|_{1}+\check{\lambda}_{n_{t}}\|\eta^{*}\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\check{\lambda}_{n_{t}}}{2}\left(\|\hat{\eta}_{n}(v)\|_{1}+\|\eta^{*}\|_{1}\right)+\check{\lambda}_{n_{t}}\|\eta^{*}\|_{1}.

Therefore,

\textstyle\frac{\check{\lambda}_{n_{t}}}{2}\|\hat{\eta}_{n}(v)\|_{1}\leq\frac{3\check{\lambda}_{n_{t}}}{2}\|\eta^{*}\|_{1}\implies\|\hat{\eta}_{n}(v)\|_{1}\leq 3\|\eta^{*}\|_{1}\leq 3C_{\eta}.

(C.10)

Applying this argument to both
\hat{\eta}_{n}(a)
and

\hat{\eta}_{n}(a^{0})
, we obtain

\textstyle\|\hat{\eta}_{n}(a)-\hat{\eta}_{n}(a^{0})\|_{1}\leq 6C_{\eta}\implies\|\hat{\eta}^{\mathrm{KKT}}(a)-\hat{\eta}^{\mathrm{KKT}}(a^{0})\|_{1}\leq\frac{6C_{\eta}}{n_{t}}\,.

Therefore, by Lemma
C.2
,

\displaystyle\|\hat{\tau}(a)-\hat{\tau}(a^{0})\|_{2}^{2}\leq\|a-a^{0}\|_{\infty}\left\|\hat{\eta}^{\mathrm{KKT}}(a)-\hat{\eta}^{\mathrm{KKT}}(a^{0})\right\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{6C_{\eta}}{n_{t}}\|a-a^{0}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle=\frac{6C_{\eta}}{n_{t}}\|\hat{m}_{c}-B_{0}\bar{X}\|_{\infty}.

Consequently, since
n\asymp n_{t}
,

\textstyle n\|\hat{\tau}(a)-\hat{\tau}(a^{0})\|_{2}^{2}\leq\frac{6C_{\eta}n}{n_{t}}\|\hat{m}_{c}-B_{0}\bar{X}\|_{\infty}=O_{p}\left(\sqrt{\frac{\log p}{n}}+\sqrt{\frac{\log q}{n}}\right)=o_{p}(1).

Hence,
\sqrt{n}\|\hat{\tau}(a)-\hat{\tau}(a^{0})\|_{2}=o_{p}(1)
, which completes the proof.
∎

C.3
Proof of Lemma
B.2

Proof.

Recall from Proposition
4.1
that we have
\hat{\theta}_{10}^{\rm cf}-\theta_{10}=\Delta_{n}^{\rm cf}+V_{n}^{\rm cf}
, where

\textstyle V_{n}^{\rm cf}=\frac{1}{2}\left[\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\in\mathcal{D}_{2}\\
A_{i}=1\end{subarray}}\hat{\tau}_{1,i}^{(2)}\epsilon_{i}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\in\mathcal{D}_{1}\\
A_{i}=0\end{subarray}}\hat{\tau}_{2,i}^{(1)}U_{i}^{\top}\gamma_{1}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\in\mathcal{D}_{1}\\
A_{i}=1\end{subarray}}\hat{\tau}_{1,i}^{(1)}\epsilon_{i}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\in\mathcal{D}_{2}\\
A_{i}=0\end{subarray}}\hat{\tau}_{2,i}^{(2)}U_{i}^{\top}\gamma_{1}\right].

For
j\in\{1,2\}
, let
\hat{\tau}_{1}^{(j),0}
denote the oracle first balancing weight obtained by replacing the random mediator target
\hat{m}_{c,j}
by
\mathbf{B}_{0}\bar{X}
(same as in the proof of Theorem
5.9
), and correspondingly define an oracle version of
V_{n}^{\rm cf}
as:

\textstyle V_{n}^{\rm cf,0}=\frac{1}{2}\left[\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\in\mathcal{D}_{2}\\
A_{i}=1\end{subarray}}\hat{\tau}_{1,i}^{(2),0}\epsilon_{i}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\in\mathcal{D}_{1}\\
A_{i}=0\end{subarray}}\hat{\tau}_{2,i}^{(1)}U_{i}^{\top}\gamma_{1}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\in\mathcal{D}_{1}\\
A_{i}=1\end{subarray}}\hat{\tau}_{1,i}^{(1),0}\epsilon_{i}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\in\mathcal{D}_{2}\\
A_{i}=0\end{subarray}}\hat{\tau}_{2,i}^{(2)}U_{i}^{\top}\gamma_{1}\right].

along with its corresponding variance scale as
(\sigma_{n}^{\rm cf,0})^{2}
defined in Equation (
B.7
). Furthermore, for notational simplicity, let us define
L_{n}^{\rm cf,0}=\sqrt{n}V_{n}^{\rm cf,0}/\sigma_{n}^{\rm cf,0}
and
T_{n}^{\rm cf}=\sqrt{n}(\hat{\theta}_{10}^{\rm cf}-\theta_{10})/\sigma_{n}^{\rm cf}
. We first establish that

T_{n}^{\rm cf}-L_{n}^{\rm cf,0}=o_{p}(1).

(C.11)

Towards that end, define
\mathcal{G}_{n}=\sigma(A_{1:n},X_{1:n},M_{1:n})
. Conditional on
\mathcal{G}_{n}
, both the actual and oracle first
balancing weights are fixed, whereas the treated outcome errors remain
centered and independent. Therefore,

\textstyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\sqrt{n}\left(V_{n}^{\rm cf}-V_{n}^{\rm cf,0}\right)\right)^{2}\,\middle|\,\mathcal{G}_{n}\right]=\frac{n\sigma_{1}^{2}}{4}\sum_{j=1}^{2}\left\|\hat{\tau}_{1}^{(j)}-\hat{\tau}_{1}^{(j),0}\right\|_{2}^{2}=o_{p}(1),

where the last conclusion follows from
Lemma
B.1
. Consequently, by a conditional version of Markov’s inequality,
\sqrt{n}\left(V_{n}^{\rm cf}-V_{n}^{\rm cf,0}\right)=o_{p}(1)
. Furthermore, we have already established in Equation (
B.8
) that
|(\sigma_{n}^{\rm cf})^{2}-(\sigma_{n}^{\rm cf,0})^{2}|=o_{p}(1)
. Since
\sigma_{n}^{\rm cf}
is bounded away from zero with probability going to one, the same conclusion holds for
\sigma_{n}^{\rm cf,0}
, and using the same logic as in for the proof of lower bounding
(\sigma_{n}^{\rm cf})^{2}
in the proof of Theorem
5.9
, we conclude:

\frac{\sigma_{n}^{\rm cf,0}}{\sigma_{n}^{\rm cf}}-1=o_{p}(1).

Now, going back to
L_{n}^{\rm cf,0}
and
T_{n}^{\rm cf}
, we have the following decomposition:

\textstyle T_{n}^{\rm cf}-L_{n}^{\rm cf,0}=\frac{\sqrt{n}\,\Delta_{n}^{\rm cf}}{\sigma_{n}^{\rm cf}}+\frac{\sqrt{n}\left(V_{n}^{\rm cf}-V_{n}^{\rm cf,0}\right)}{\sigma_{n}^{\rm cf}}+L_{n}^{\rm cf,0}\left(\frac{\sigma_{n}^{\rm cf,0}}{\sigma_{n}^{\rm cf}}-1\right).

The first term is
o_{p}(1)
by the bias part of the proof of Theorem
5.9
; the second term is
o_{p}(1)
by the preceding calculation; and the third term is
o_{p}(1)
because
L_{n}^{\rm cf,0}=O_{p}(1)
and
\sigma_{n}^{\rm cf,0}/\sigma_{n}^{\rm cf}-1=o_{p}(1)
. This proves (
C.11
).
It remains to establish the conditional asymptotic normality of
L_{n}^{\rm cf,0}
. Define
\mathcal{H}_{n}=\sigma\left(A_{1:n},X_{1:n},\{M_{i}:A_{i}=1\}\right).

Then
\mathcal{X}_{n}\subseteq\mathcal{H}_{n}
. Conditional on
\mathcal{H}_{n}
, the oracle first balancing weights
\{\hat{\tau}_{1}^{(j),0}\}_{j=1}^{2}
, the control balancing weights
\{\hat{\tau}_{2}^{(j)}\}_{j=1}^{2}
, and
\sigma_{n}^{\rm cf,0}
are fixed. Moreover, the treated outcome errors and the control mediator errors in
V_{n}^{\rm cf,0}
are conditionally centered and independent across
i
. Now, define the triangular-array summands

\xi_{n,i}^{0}=\frac{\sqrt{n}}{2\sigma_{n}^{\rm cf,0}}\begin{cases}\hat{\tau}_{1,i}^{(2),0}\epsilon_{i},&i\in\mathcal{D}_{2},\ A_{i}=1,\\[2.77501pt]
\hat{\tau}_{2,i}^{(1)}U_{i}^{\top}\gamma_{1},&i\in\mathcal{D}_{1},\ A_{i}=0,\\[2.77501pt]
\hat{\tau}_{1,i}^{(1),0}\epsilon_{i},&i\in\mathcal{D}_{1},\ A_{i}=1,\\[2.77501pt]
\hat{\tau}_{2,i}^{(2)}U_{i}^{\top}\gamma_{1},&i\in\mathcal{D}_{2},\ A_{i}=0.\end{cases}

Then, it is immediate that
L_{n}^{\rm cf,0}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\xi_{n,i}^{0}
and we also have
\mathbb{E}[\xi_{n,i}^{0}\mid\mathcal{H}_{n}]=0
, along with

\textstyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left[(\xi_{n,i}^{0})^{2}\,\middle|\,\mathcal{H}_{n}\right]=\frac{n}{4(\sigma_{n}^{\rm cf,0})^{2}}\sum_{j=1}^{2}\left\{\sigma_{1}^{2}\|\hat{\tau}_{1}^{(j),0}\|_{2}^{2}+\gamma_{1}^{\top}\Sigma_{U,0}\gamma_{1}\|\hat{\tau}_{2}^{(j)}\|_{2}^{2}\right\}=1.

We next verify the conditional Lyapunov condition. By the uniform conditional sub-Gaussian assumptions on
\epsilon_{i}
and
U_{i}
, and the boundedness of
\|\gamma_{1}\|_{2}
, there exists a constant
C>0
such that

\textstyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left[|\xi_{n,i}^{0}|^{3}\,\middle|\,\mathcal{H}_{n}\right]\leq(Cn^{3/2}/(\sigma_{n}^{\rm cf,0})^{3})\sum_{j=1}^{2}\{\|\hat{\tau}_{1}^{(j),0}\|_{\infty}\|\hat{\tau}_{1}^{(j),0}\|_{2}^{2}+\|\hat{\tau}_{2}^{(j)}\|_{\infty}\|\hat{\tau}_{2}^{(j)}\|_{2}^{2}\}.

From Lemma
5.8
,
Lemma
B.1
, and the constraints in optimization problems Equation (
3.1
) and (
3.2
), we have
n\|\hat{\tau}_{1}^{(j),0}\|_{2}^{2}+n\|\hat{\tau}_{2}^{(j)}\|_{2}^{2}=O_{p}(1)
and
\|\hat{\tau}_{1}^{(j),0}\|_{\infty}\vee\|\hat{\tau}_{2}^{(j)}\|_{\infty}=O_{p}(n^{-\alpha})
.
Since
\sigma_{n}^{\rm cf,0}
is bounded away from zero with probability going to one, it follows that

\textstyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left[|\xi_{n,i}^{0}|^{3}\,\middle|\,\mathcal{H}_{n}\right]=O_{p}(n^{1/2-\alpha})=o_{p}(1),

where the last conclusion follows from
\alpha>1/2
. Therefore, by the conditional Lyapunov central limit theorem, for every
s\in{\mathbb{R}}
,

\textstyle\mathbb{E}\left[e^{isL_{n}^{\rm cf,0}}\,\middle|\,\mathcal{H}_{n}\right]\longrightarrow e^{-s^{2}/2},\hskip 18.49988pt\text{in probability}.

(C.12)

To go towards
\mathcal{X}_{n}
from
\mathcal{H}_{n}
, by law of total expectation,

\textstyle\mathbb{E}\left[e^{isL_{n}^{\rm cf,0}}\,\middle|\,\mathcal{X}_{n}\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}\left(e^{isL_{n}^{\rm cf,0}}\,\middle|\,\mathcal{H}_{n}\right)\,\middle|\,\mathcal{X}_{n}\right].

Now, define

\textstyle Z_{n}(s)=\left|\mathbb{E}\left(e^{isL_{n}^{\rm cf,0}}\,\middle|\,\mathcal{H}_{n}\right)-e^{-s^{2}/2}\right|.

By (
C.12
),
Z_{n}(s)\to 0
in probability, and
0\leq Z_{n}(s)\leq 2
. Consequently,

Z_{n}(s)\to 0
in
L_{1}
. Hence,

\textstyle\left|\mathbb{E}\left[e^{isL_{n}^{\rm cf,0}}\,\middle|\,\mathcal{X}_{n}\right]-e^{-s^{2}/2}\right|\leq\mathbb{E}\left[Z_{n}(s)\,\middle|\,\mathcal{X}_{n}\right]\longrightarrow 0,\hskip 9.24994pt\text{in probability},

where the last conclusion follows from Markov’s inequality and

\mathbb{E}[Z_{n}(s)]\to 0
. Finally, define
D_{n}=T_{n}^{\rm cf}-L_{n}^{\rm cf,0}
. We have already established that
D_{n}=o_{p}(1)
. Therefore, for any
s\in{\mathbb{R}}
,

\displaystyle\left|\mathbb{E}\left[e^{isT_{n}^{\rm cf}}\,\middle|\,\mathcal{X}_{n}\right]-\mathbb{E}\left[e^{isL_{n}^{\rm cf,0}}\,\middle|\,\mathcal{X}_{n}\right]\right|

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\left|e^{isD_{n}}-1\right|\,\middle|\,\mathcal{X}_{n}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\min\{2,|s||D_{n}|\}\,\middle|\,\mathcal{X}_{n}\right]\longrightarrow 0,\hskip 9.24994pt\text{in probability}.

Combining the last two displays, we conclude that, for every

s\in{\mathbb{R}}
,

\textstyle\mathbb{E}\left[e^{isT_{n}^{\rm cf}}\,\middle|\,\mathcal{X}_{n}\right]\longrightarrow e^{-s^{2}/2},\hskip 18.49988pt\text{in probability}.

Since the limiting characteristic function is continuous at the origin, the conditional laws of
T_{n}^{\rm cf}
given
\mathcal{X}_{n}
converge weakly in probability to the standard normal distribution. Since
\Phi
is continuous, this implies that, for every

t\in{\mathbb{R}}
,

\textstyle\mathbb{P}\left(T_{n}^{\rm cf}\leq t\,\middle|\,\mathcal{X}_{n}\right)\longrightarrow\Phi(t),\hskip 18.49988pt\text{in probability}.

This completes the proof.
∎

Lemma C.3
.

Under Assumption
5.3
and
5.4
, the estimate
\hat{b}
, as obtained in Step 4 of Algorithm
1
satisfies:

\textstyle\|\hat{b}-b\|_{1}=O_{p}\left(sk_{\gamma}\sqrt{\frac{\log{p}}{n}}+k\sqrt{\frac{\log{p+q}}{n}}\right)\,.

Proof.

For simplicity of presentation, we will omit the fold number here. We define the response
\check{\mathbf{y}}_{c}=\mathbf{M}_{c}\hat{\gamma}_{1}
(the responses used in Step 4 of Algorithm
1
). We start from the basic inequality. From the definition of
\hat{b}
, we have:

\textstyle\hat{b}={\arg\min}_{\tilde{b}}\left\{\frac{1}{2n_{c}}\|\check{\mathbf{y}}_{c}-\mathbf{X}_{c}\tilde{b}\|_{2}^{2}+\lambda\|\tilde{b}\|_{1}\right\}

Hence, we have:

\textstyle\frac{1}{2n_{c}}\|\check{\mathbf{y}}_{c}-\mathbf{X}_{c}\hat{b}\|_{2}^{2}+\lambda\|\hat{b}\|_{1}\leq\frac{1}{2n_{c}}\|\check{\mathbf{y}}_{c}-\mathbf{X}_{c}b\|_{2}^{2}+\lambda\|b\|_{1}

Defining
\Delta=\hat{b}-b
and using the fact that
\check{\mathbf{y}}_{c}=\mathbf{X}_{c}b+\mathbf{U}_{c}\hat{\gamma}_{1}
, we have by some simple algebra:

\textstyle\frac{1}{2n_{c}}\|\mathbf{X}_{c}\Delta\|_{2}^{2}+\lambda\|\hat{b}\|_{1}\leq\left\|\frac{1}{n_{c}}\mathbf{X}_{c}^{\top}\mathbf{U}_{c}\hat{\gamma}_{1}\right\|_{\infty}\|\Delta\|_{1}+\lambda\|b\|_{1}\,.

(C.13)

Now, suppose we choose
\lambda
such that

\textstyle\mathbb{P}\left(\left\|\frac{1}{n_{c}}\mathbf{X}_{c}^{\top}\mathbf{U}_{c}\hat{\gamma}_{1}\right\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{\lambda}{2}\right)\longrightarrow 1,\hskip 9.24994pt\text{as }n\uparrow\infty\,.

(C.14)

We will later show that one can take
\lambda=K_{1}\sqrt{\log{p}/n_{c}}
for some suitably chosen large constant
K_{1}
. Let us call the above event
\Omega_{n,1}
. Then on
\Omega_{n,1}
, we have, from Equation (
C.13
),

\textstyle\frac{1}{2n_{c}}\|\mathbf{X}_{c}\Delta\|_{2}^{2}\leq\frac{\lambda}{2}\|\Delta\|_{1}+\lambda(\|b\|_{1}-\|b+\Delta\|_{1})\,.

(C.15)

Now, let
S
denotes that active set of
b_{0}=\mathbf{B}_{0}^{\top}\gamma_{1}
. Then we have
|S|\leq sk_{\gamma}
. Furthermore,

\displaystyle\|b\|_{1}-\|b+\Delta\|_{1}

\displaystyle=\|b_{S}\|_{1}-\|b_{S}+\Delta_{S}\|_{1}+\|b_{S^{c}}\|_{1}-\|b_{S^{c}}+\Delta_{S^{c}}\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\|\Delta_{S}\|_{1}+\|b_{S^{c}}\|_{1}-(\|\Delta_{S^{c}}\|_{1}-\|b_{S^{c}}\|_{1})

Hence, using this in Equation (
C.15
), along with the fact that
(\lambda/2)\|\Delta\|_{1}=(\lambda/2)\|\Delta_{S}\|_{1}+(\lambda/2)\|\Delta_{S^{c}}\|_{1})

we have:

\textstyle\frac{1}{2n_{c}}\|\mathbf{X}_{c}\Delta\|_{2}^{2}+\frac{\lambda}{2}\|\Delta_{S^{c}}\|_{1}\leq\frac{3\lambda}{2}\|\Delta_{S}\|_{1}+2\lambda\|b_{S^{c}}\|_{1}\,.

(C.16)

As
\|\mathbf{X}_{c}\Delta\|_{2}^{2}\geq 0
, it is immediate from Equation (
C.16
)

\textstyle\|\Delta_{S^{c}}\|_{1}\leq 3\|\Delta_{S}\|_{1}+4\|b_{S^{c}}\|_{1}\,.\vskip-9.24994pt

(C.17)

We divide the rest of the analysis into two cases, depending on whether
\|\Delta_{S}\|_{1}
is larger than
\|b_{S^{c}}\|_{1}
or not:

Case 1:
4\|b_{S^{c}}\|_{1}\leq 3\|\Delta_{S}\|_{1}
.
In this case, we have from Equation (
C.17
),
\|\Delta_{S^{c}}\|_{1}\leq 6\|\Delta_{S}\|_{1}
. Also from Equation (
C.16
), we have:

\textstyle\frac{1}{2n_{c}}\|\mathbf{X}_{c}\Delta\|_{2}^{2}+\frac{\lambda}{2}\|\Delta_{S^{c}}\|_{1}\leq 3\lambda\|\Delta_{S}\|_{1}

(C.18)

Furthermore, the compatibility condition holds, and define the event
\Omega_{n,2}
, on which
\mathbf{X}_{c}
satisfies the RE condition. Then by Assumption
5.3
and Lemma
C.1
,
\mathbb{P}(\Omega_{n,2})\to 1
as
n\uparrow\infty
and consequently, on
\Omega_{n,2}
, we have for some constant
\phi_{0}
:

\textstyle\frac{\|\mathbf{X}_{c}\Delta\|_{2}^{2}}{n_{c}}\geq\phi_{0}\|\Delta\|_{2}^{2}\implies\|\Delta_{S}\|_{1}\leq\sqrt{sk_{\gamma}}\|\Delta_{S}\|_{2}\leq\sqrt{sk_{\gamma}}\|\Delta\|_{2}\leq\sqrt{sk_{\gamma}}\frac{\|\mathbf{X}_{c}\Delta\|_{2}}{\sqrt{n_{c}}\sqrt{\phi_{0}}}\,.

(C.19)

Hence, from Equation (
C.18
), we have:

\textstyle\frac{1}{2n_{c}}\|\mathbf{X}_{c}\Delta\|_{2}^{2}+\frac{\lambda}{2}\|\Delta_{S^{c}}\|_{1}\leq 3\lambda\sqrt{sk_{\gamma}}\frac{\|\mathbf{X}_{c}\Delta\|_{2}}{\sqrt{n_{c}}\sqrt{\phi_{0}}}\leq\frac{1}{4n_{c}}\|\mathbf{X}_{c}\Delta\|_{2}^{2}+\frac{9}{\phi_{0}}sk_{\gamma}\lambda^{2}

(C.20)

Hence, changing sides, yields:

\textstyle\frac{1}{n_{c}}\|\mathbf{X}_{c}\Delta\|_{2}^{2}\leq\frac{36}{\phi_{0}}sk_{\gamma}\lambda^{2}\implies\frac{1}{\sqrt{n_{c}}}\|\mathbf{X}_{c}\Delta\|_{2}\leq\frac{6}{\sqrt{\phi_{0}}}\lambda\sqrt{sk_{\gamma}}

This, along with Equation (
C.19
) implies
\|\Delta_{S}\|_{1}\leq(6\lambda sk_{\gamma})/\phi_{0}
and consequently, using the fact under this case 1
\|\Delta_{S^{c}}\|_{1}\leq 6\|\Delta_{S}\|_{1}
, we conclude that:

\textstyle\|\Delta_{1}\|_{1}=\|\Delta_{S}\|_{1}+\|\Delta_{S^{c}}\|_{1}\leq 7\|\Delta_{S}\|_{1}\leq\frac{42}{\phi_{0}}\lambda sk_{\gamma}=O_{p}\left(sk_{\gamma}\sqrt{\frac{\log{p}}{n}}\right)\,.

(C.21)

Here in the last line we have used the fact
\lambda=K_{1}\sqrt{\log{p}/n_{c}}
, a fact that we will prove at the end, and
n_{t}\sim n_{c}\sim n
.

Case 2:
4\|b_{S^{c}}\|_{1}>3\|\Delta_{S}\|_{1}
:
In this case, we have from Equation (
C.16
),

\textstyle\frac{1}{2n_{c}}\|\mathbf{X}_{c}\Delta\|_{2}^{2}+\frac{\lambda}{2}\|\Delta_{S^{c}}\|_{1}\leq 4\lambda\|b_{S^{c}}\|_{1}\implies\|\Delta_{S^{c}}\|_{1}\leq 8\|b_{S^{c}}\|_{1}\implies\|\Delta\|_{1}\leq\frac{28}{3}\|b_{S^{c}}\|_{1}\,.

(C.22)

Now,
\|b_{S^{c}}\|_{1}=\|(b-b_{0})_{S^{c}}\|_{1}\leq\|b-b_{0}\|_{1}
, where in the first equality, we have used the fact that
(b_{0})_{S^{c}}\equiv 0
by definition of
S
. On the other have, we have:

\textstyle\|b-b_{0}\|_{1}=\|\mathbf{B}_{0}^{\top}(\hat{\gamma}_{1}-\gamma_{1})\|_{1}\leq\|\mathbf{B}_{0}\|_{1,\infty}\|\hat{\gamma}_{1}-\gamma_{1}\|_{1}=O_{p}\left(k\sqrt{\frac{\log{(p+q)}}{n}}\right)

where the last conclusion follows from the fact that
\|\mathbf{B}_{0}\|_{1,\infty}\leq C_{\mathbf{B}}<\infty
and
\|\hat{\gamma}_{1}-\gamma_{1}\|_{1}\leq\|\hat{\phi}_{1}-\phi_{1}\|_{1}=O_{p}(k\sqrt{\log{(p+q)/n}})
. This completes the proof. Now, all we need to show is that
\lambda=K_{1}\sqrt{\log{p}/n_{c}}
is a valid choice for Equation (
C.14
) to hold. As
\hat{\gamma}_{1}
is obtained from separate data (treatment data), and
U_{i}
’s are sub-Gaussian conditional on
X
, it is immediate from the sub-Gaussian concentration inequality that for a large enough constant
C
,

\textstyle\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{1}{n_{c}\|\hat{\gamma}_{1}\|_{2}}\|\mathbf{X}_{c}^{\top}\mathbf{U}_{c}\hat{\gamma}_{1}\|_{\infty}>C\sqrt{\frac{\log{p}}{n_{c}}}\mid\hat{\gamma}_{1}\right)\to 0,\hskip 9.24994pt\text{ as }n\uparrow\infty\,.

Consequently
\|\mathbf{X}_{c}^{\top}\mathbf{U}_{c}\hat{\gamma}_{1}\|_{\infty}/n_{c}\leq C\|\hat{\gamma}_{1}\|_{2}\sqrt{\log{p}/n_{c}}
with probability going to
1
. Now
\|\hat{\gamma}_{1}\|_{2}\leq\|\gamma_{1}\|_{2}+\|\hat{\gamma}_{1}-\gamma_{1}\|_{2}\leq 1+C_{\gamma}
with probability going to
1
as
\|\hat{\gamma}_{1}-\gamma_{1}\|_{2}\leq\|\hat{\phi}_{1}-\phi_{1}\|_{2}=o_{p}(1)
which follows from the consistency of the lasso estimator. Therefore,
\|\mathbf{X}_{c}^{\top}\mathbf{U}_{c}\hat{\gamma}_{1}\|_{\infty}/n_{c}\leq C(1+C_{\gamma})\sqrt{\log{p}/n_{c}}
. Taking
K_{1}=2C(1+C_{\gamma})
would conclude the proof.
∎

C.4
Calculation of Effects

By taking expectations of the outcome and mediator regressions conditional on
(X,M,A)
and
(X,A)
, respectively, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[Y(1,M(1))]

\displaystyle=\alpha_{1}+\mathbb{E}[X]^{\top}\beta_{1}+\mathbb{E}\!\left[\mathbb{E}[M\mid X,A=1]\right]^{\top}\gamma_{1}

\displaystyle=(\alpha_{1}+\delta_{1}^{\top}\gamma_{1})+\mathbb{E}[X]^{\top}(\beta_{1}+\mathbf{B}_{1}^{\top}\gamma_{1}),

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[Y(0,M(0))]

\displaystyle=\alpha_{0}+\mathbb{E}[X]^{\top}\beta_{0}+\mathbb{E}\!\left[\mathbb{E}[M\mid X,A=0]\right]^{\top}\gamma_{0}

\displaystyle=(\alpha_{0}+\delta_{0}^{\top}\gamma_{0})+\mathbb{E}[X]^{\top}(\beta_{0}+\mathbf{B}_{0}^{\top}\gamma_{0}),

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[Y(1,M(0))]

\displaystyle=\alpha_{1}+\mathbb{E}[X]^{\top}\beta_{1}+\mathbb{E}\!\left[\mathbb{E}[M\mid X,A=0]\right]^{\top}\gamma_{1}

\displaystyle=(\alpha_{1}+\delta_{0}^{\top}\gamma_{1})+\mathbb{E}[X]^{\top}(\beta_{1}+\mathbf{B}_{0}^{\top}\gamma_{1}),

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[Y(0,M(1))]

\displaystyle=\alpha_{0}+\mathbb{E}[X]^{\top}\beta_{0}+\mathbb{E}\!\left[\mathbb{E}[M\mid X,A=1]\right]^{\top}\gamma_{0}

\displaystyle=(\alpha_{0}+\delta_{1}^{\top}\gamma_{0})+\mathbb{E}[X]^{\top}(\beta_{0}+\mathbf{B}_{1}^{\top}\gamma_{0}).

Therefore, the natural indirect effect simplifies as

\displaystyle\mathrm{NIE}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[Y(1,M(1))]-\mathbb{E}[Y(1,M(0))]

\displaystyle=(\alpha_{1}+\delta_{1}^{\top}\gamma_{1})+\mathbb{E}[X]^{\top}(\beta_{1}+\mathbf{B}_{1}^{\top}\gamma_{1})-(\alpha_{1}+\delta_{0}^{\top}\gamma_{1})-\mathbb{E}[X]^{\top}(\beta_{1}+\mathbf{B}_{0}^{\top}\gamma_{1})
